# eMailSuperSearch PRD feasibility research (2026-10-23)

Raw output of a 15-agent research sweep: seven research areas, each independently re-verified by a skeptic agent, followed by a completeness critic. Every package fact was checked against PyPI's JSON API on 2026-10-23. **These are findings and recommendations, not decisions.** The design document will record what the owner actually decides.

Constraints the sweep was run against: pip-only delivery on PyPI, modular extras, offline web UI with projects and a left search pane, corporate Windows 11 laptop with a standard (non-admin) user, zero telemetry, everything in the user profile, background 1:1 mirror of a .msg folder.

Target hardware: Ryzen 5 PRO 8640HS, Radeon 760M iGPU, 16 GB shared RAM.



---

## Area: msg-parsing-sync-export


### Summary

extract-msg (0.56.1, 2026-08-14, pure-Python, GPL-3.0) is the only maintained open-source .msg parser and is adequate: it exposes sender/to/cc/bcc, tz-aware local-time dates, subject, plain/HTML/RTF bodies with automatic RTF de-encapsulation fallbacks, nested .msg attachments, headers, Message-ID and In-Reply-To; ConversationIndex/ConversationTopic are not surfaced as properties but are reachable via getStream('__substg1.0_00710102') / getStringStream('__substg1.0_0070'). The PRD's filename-set sync is unsafe because Outlook drag-drop names files by subject, stamps mtime with the drop time, and re-exports are byte-different, so identity must come from Message-ID (or a metadata hash) in a SQLite manifest keyed by path with (file_id, size, mtime_ns, content hash); watchdog can accelerate but silently loses events on buffer overflow, so periodic full reconciliation is mandatory. The PRD's one-vector-per-email design is broken by construction: Chroma's default all-MiniLM-L6-v2 ONNX function truncates at 256 word pieces, so appended attachment text is never embedded; chunk at ~200-256 tokens with overlap, index chunks, and return parent emails via metadata grouping. SQLite FTS5 is compiled into python.org Windows builds (verified locally on 3.14 / SQLite 3.50.4) and gives BM25 plus trigram substring search for invoice numbers and names for free; fuse with vectors via RRF (k=60). openpyxl hyperlinks with Path.as_uri() work for local and UNC paths (verified), but every click on a file:// .msg link triggers Office's hyperlink warning dialog unless a user-level HKCU registry value is set, and the opening app depends on whether classic or new Outlook owns the .msg association.


### Recommended

Keep extract-msg (0.56.x, pure Python) behind a small adapter that adds conversation_index/topic, normalizes dates to UTC epoch ints, and never leaks file handles. Replace the filename-set sync with a SQLite manifest keyed by path with (file_id, size, mtime_ns, sha256, Message-ID-or-metadata-hash), driven by a periodic full reconciliation scan with two-phase deletes, and use watchdog (optional extra) only as a debounced rescan trigger. Index 200-250-token overlapping chunks (body and each attachment separately, with header context prepended) in Chroma plus an FTS5 external-content index in the same SQLite file, fuse BM25 and kNN with RRF (k=60) and group back to parent emails for display. Export with openpyxl directly (no pandas), Path.as_uri() hyperlinks styled 'Hyperlink', a plain path column, and an in-app os.startfile 'Open in Outlook' button as the primary action because Office will show a security prompt on every file:// click.


### Alternatives

  - Chroma native hybrid search (Search/Knn/Rrf + Bm25EmbeddingFunction) — fewer moving parts if it works with PersistentClient, but local availability is undocumented (all docs are under /cloud/) and it ties ranking to Chroma's roadmap.
  - bm25s 0.3.15 (2026-10-22, numpy-based, persistable index) or rank-bm25 0.2.2 (2022, in-memory) — simple Python BM25 without SQLite, but no incremental updates (bm25s rebuilds the index) and no substring/trigram matching.
  - (size, mtime_ns) only, no content hash — fastest scan, but misses same-second overwrites and cannot recognize re-exported duplicates; acceptable only if hashing is added lazily for changed rows.
  - Full content hash every scan — catches everything but reads every file each cycle; too slow for tens of thousands of .msg on a laptop/SMB share.
  - watchdog-only live sync without reconciliation — lowest latency, but events are silently dropped on buffer overflow and unreliable on network shares, so the 1:1 mirror guarantee is lost.
  - Aspose.Email for Python via .NET — commercial, broadest MAPI/PST support, but 58 MB, proprietary EULA, Python <3.14 only; conflicts with 'pip-only, free' goals.
  - libpff-python PST/OST ingestion as an optional extra — pip-installable Windows wheels exist, but alpha-status parser; keep out of v1.
  - Single vector per email (PRD) — simplest, but provably discards all text past 256 word pieces including attachments.
  - xlsxwriter instead of openpyxl for export — not researched here; openpyxl is already needed if any .xlsx reading is planned.


### Install Footprint

Recommended set for this area (verified wheel sizes from PyPI JSON): extract-msg 0.34 MB + olefile 0.11 MB + RTFDE 0.04 MB (+lark, oletools 0.99 MB) + compressed-rtf 0.01 MB + ebcdic 0.13 MB + red-black-tree-mod 0.03 MB (sdist) + tzlocal + beautifulsoup4 (not measured, ~0.2 MB) + openpyxl 0.25 MB + watchdog 0.08 MB (optional) + sqlite3 stdlib = roughly 2-3 MB. Shared with the PRD: chromadb 1.5.9 win_amd64 wheel 23.5 MB plus its deps (onnxruntime, tokenizers, grpcio, kubernetes, opentelemetry — total not measured, expect well over 100 MB installed) plus the default all-MiniLM-L6-v2 ONNX download at first run (size not verified; the model is ~80-90 MB on Hugging Face). PRD-only extras this area would drop: pandas (not measured, ~11-12 MB wheel) if export uses openpyxl directly. Optional PST extra: libpff-python 0.37 MB wheel. Commercial alternative for comparison: Aspose.Email 58.2 MB wheel.


### Open Questions For Owner

  - extract-msg is GPL-3.0: is a GPL dependency acceptable for a library you publish on PyPI (it constrains your own license), or must the parser be isolated/replaced?
  - Do the target archive folders live on local NTFS, on OneDrive/SharePoint-synced folders, or on UNC network shares? This decides watchdog vs polling, file-id reliability, and whether exported hyperlinks work for other people.
  - Which Outlook owns the .msg file association on the office laptops (classic Outlook vs new Outlook, which needs an M365 subscription and had a 14 MB limit)? Should 'open original' go through Excel links at all, or only via the web UI's 'Open in Outlook' button?
  - Is modifying the user's own HKCU registry (DisableHyperlinkWarning) acceptable under corporate policy if the user explicitly opts in, or must the product live with one security prompt per link click?
  - Is PST/OST import (via alpha-quality libpff-python) in scope for v1, or is .msg-folder ingestion the only source?
  - What staleness is acceptable for the 1:1 mirror (seconds via watchdog, or minutes via periodic scan)? This sets the reconciliation interval and battery/IO budget.
  - Should nested .msg attachments and attachments-of-attachments be indexed as separate searchable records (own row, own hyperlink to the parent file) or merged into the parent email?
  - Maximum attachment size to parse/OCR and a per-file time budget: what should happen to oversized or failing files (skip with a UI 'not indexed' flag?)
  - Will exported .xlsx files be shared with colleagues (Protected View / unreachable local paths), or are they personal working files only?


### Claims

  1. [high] extract-msg latest is 0.56.1 uploaded 2026-08-14 as a pure-Python wheel (extract_msg-0.56.1-py3-none-any.whl, 338 KB, requires Python >=3.8), 130 releases total, repo pushed 2026-10-23, 855 stars, 22 open issues, license GPL-3.0. Runtime deps: olefile==0.47, tzlocal>=4.2,<6, compressed-rtf, ebcdic, beautifulsoup4, RTFDE<0.2, red-black-tree-mod (sdist only, pure Python). Extras: [encoding]=chardet, [image]=Pillow<10 (stale pin), [mime]=python-magic.
     -> Pin extract-msg>=0.56,<0.57 (project is 0.x and README says anything MAY change between minors). Do not install the [image] extra (Pillow<10 conflicts with modern stacks). GPL-3.0 dependency is a licensing decision for a PyPI-distributed library (see open questions).
  2. [high] MessageBase exposes: sender, to, cc, bcc (str), recipients (List[Recipient]), date and receivedTime (datetime), subject, body (str), htmlBody (bytes), htmlBodyPrepared, rtfBody, compressedRtf, deencapsulatedRtf, header (email.message.Message built from the transport-header stream or synthesized), headerDict, messageId, inReplyTo, parsedDate, isRead, isSent, attachments. body falls back to RTFDE plain-text de-encapsulation when PR_BODY (__substg1.0_1000) is absent; htmlBody falls back RTF->HTML and then plain-text->HTML. messageId returns the header Message-ID first, else PR_INTERNET_MESSAGE_ID (__substg1.0_1035); inReplyTo reads __substg1.0_1042.
     -> Read body via msg.body (already handles RTF-only mail); keep htmlBody only for the UI preview. Use messageId as the primary logical identity; recipients list gives per-recipient type/email for metadata arrays.
  3. [high] conversationIndex, conversationTopic and References are NOT exposed as properties in extract-msg (grep of message_base.py/msg.py finds no 'conversation'). They are reachable through generic accessors: msg.getStream('__substg1.0_00710102') for PidTagConversationIndex (binary) and msg.getStringStream('__substg1.0_0070') for PidTagConversationTopic (getStringStream appends 001F/001E itself), or msg.header['References'].
     -> Wrap extract-msg in a thin adapter that adds conversation_index, conversation_topic and references so threading does not depend on undocumented stream names scattered through the code.
  4. [high] PidTagConversationIndex = 22-byte header (1 reserved byte 0x01, 5-byte big-endian truncated FILETIME, 16-byte GUID generated per new thread) followed by one 5-byte block per reply level; each reply extends the parent's index, so all messages in a thread share the same first 22 bytes and a child's index has the parent's as a prefix.
     -> Store thread_id = hex(conv_index[6:22]) (the GUID) and depth = (len-22)//5 as metadata; group search hits by thread, order by index length, and use parent-prefix matching to reconstruct reply trees without relying on In-Reply-To (often missing in internal Exchange mail).
  5. [high] extract-msg dates are tz-aware in the machine's LOCAL zone: props.date reads PR_CLIENT_SUBMIT_TIME (00390040), converted by utils.fromTimeStamp -> datetime.fromtimestamp(stamp, tzlocal.get_localzone()); receivedTime reads PR_MESSAGE_DELIVERY_TIME (0E060040). date returns None when the message is not flagged as sent (isSent False, e.g. drafts). Far-future FILETIME values map to NullDate sentinels.
     -> Normalize to int(dt.astimezone(UTC).timestamp()) before storing (Chroma range filters need ints); fallback chain date -> receivedTime -> header Date -> file mtime, and flag which source was used. Treat NullDate as missing.
  6. [high] Encoding handling: Unicode stores decode as utf-16-le; for non-Unicode stores extract-msg reads PR_INTERNET_CPID (3FFD0003) and maps it to a codepage, and if absent logs a warning and defaults to iso-8859-15. openMsg accepts overrideEncoding='chardet' (needs the chardet extra) and errorBehavior/delayAttachments kwargs; 0.54.1 fixed a child-MSG/parent encoding mismatch and 0.56.1 fixed an HTML-body decoding error.
     -> Install extract-msg[encoding]; on UnicodeDecodeError retry with overrideEncoding='chardet'; never let one bad file abort a batch; record parse errors per file in the manifest for a UI 'problem files' list.
  7. [medium] RTF de-encapsulation (RTFDE) is a known failure point: the CLI has an explicit --ignore-rtfde flag, RTFDE's own notes say it fully unquotes de-encapsulated text, and 0.54.1 had to speed it up by stripping data before passing to RTFDE. RTFDE 0.1.2.2 (2025-12-09) depends on lark~=1.3 and oletools>=0.56.
     -> Catch exceptions around msg.body/htmlBody; as a last resort strip control words from rtfBody with a regex so RTF-only mail still gets indexed; run parsing in a worker process with a per-file timeout.
  8. [high] Attachments: msg.attachments returns AttachmentBase objects with .type (AttachmentType.DATA, MSG, WEB, SIGNED, SIGNED_EMBEDDED, BROKEN, UNSUPPORTED, UNKNOWN); EmbeddedMsgAttachment.data returns a full MSGFile for nested .msg; Attachment.data returns bytes. getStream reads the entire OLE stream into memory (stream.read()), so large attachments are fully materialized. MSGFile supports the context manager protocol (__enter__/__exit__) and 0.55.0 fixed a leaked OLE handle in openMsg.
     -> Always use 'with extract_msg.openMsg(path, delayAttachments=True) as msg' so the file handle is released (otherwise Windows blocks the user's deletes/moves and the sync sees phantom files). Recurse into AttachmentType.MSG with a depth cap; skip attachments above a configurable size (e.g. 25 MB) and record them as 'not indexed'.
  9. [medium] The same email exported twice from Outlook produces byte-different .msg files (differing checksums) because the OLE storage carries timestamps and property ordering; copying an exported file does yield identical bytes. eDiscovery practice therefore dedupes on metadata fields, not file hash.
     -> Content hash of the file detects 'changed on disk' but cannot detect 'same email saved again'. Use a logical key: Message-ID when present, else sha256(normalized sender | sent_ts | subject | first 2 KB of body). Keep both keys in the manifest.
  10. [medium] Outlook drag-drop to a folder names the file '<subject>.msg', sets the file's modified date to the time of the drag (Microsoft support engineer: 'It's by design'), and a second message with the same subject blocks/overwrites rather than being auto-suffixed; the new Outlook reportedly refuses the transfer entirely. Explorer's '(2)' suffix is not automatic.
     -> Never derive identity or date from filename or mtime. Expect many 'RE: ...msg' names and user renames; the manifest must map path -> logical key and tolerate the same logical key appearing under several paths (store all paths, index once).
  11. [high] Filename-set diff (PRD 4.1) cannot detect in-place replacement under the same name, treats renames/moves as delete+add (full re-parse and re-embed), and cannot detect truncated or partially written files. os.stat(path) on Windows returns the NTFS file index in st_ino (since Python 3.5; up to 128-bit since 3.12) which is stable across rename (verified locally: identical st_ino before/after os.rename), and st_mtime_ns gives 100 ns resolution.
     -> Manifest row per path: (path PK, volume_id=st_dev, file_id=st_ino, size, mtime_ns, file_sha256, message_key, parse_status, last_seen_scan_id). Change detection: (size, mtime_ns) differ -> rehash -> reparse only if hash changed; path gone but (volume_id,file_id) reappears elsewhere -> rename: update path metadata only, no re-embedding.
  12. [high] Robust deletion detection is a full enumeration diff: a scan stamps every seen row with scan_id; rows whose last_seen_scan_id < current are tombstoned and their chunks removed from the vector store and FTS. ReadDirectoryChangesW itself tells callers to 'compute the changes by enumerating the directory' whenever its buffer overflows.
     -> Run the full scan on startup and on a timer (e.g. every 15 min, configurable); make deletion two-phase (tombstone, then purge after the next scan confirms) so a transient unmount/offline share does not wipe the index.
  13. [high] watchdog 6.0.0 (2024-11-01, py3-none-win_amd64 wheel 79 KB, requires >=3.9; master README now says 3.11+) uses ReadDirectoryChangesW with a 64000-byte buffer. When the OS buffer overflows, ReadDirectoryChangesW returns success with zero bytes and discards all pending changes; watchdog simply slices event_buffer.raw[:0] and has no ERROR_NOTIFY_ENUM_DIR handling (0 matches in winapi.py), so bursts (e.g. dropping 500 emails) can silently lose events. Renames arrive as RENAMED_OLD_NAME/NEW_NAME pairs that watchdog stitches into a moved event; the Windows API rejects buffers >64 KB on network paths and may not work at all on some redirectors, for which watchdog recommends PollingObserver.
     -> Use watchdog only as a debounced trigger ('something changed under folder X, rescan X in 2 s'); never apply its events directly to the index. Auto-select PollingObserver when the archive root is a UNC/mapped network path. Make watchdog an optional extra; the periodic scan alone must satisfy the 1:1 mirror requirement.
  14. [high] all-MiniLM-L6-v2 is 'a sentence and short paragraph encoder' whose inputs 'longer than 256 word pieces' are truncated by default and which was trained with sequence length 128; Chroma's default ONNXMiniLM_L6_V2 embedding function hard-codes tokenizer.enable_truncation(max_length=256).
     -> PRD 4.2 'append all attachment text to the body and embed once' silently discards everything after roughly the first 180-200 words: attachments and most of any long email are never searchable. Chunking is mandatory, not an optimization.
  15. [high] Chroma's default embedding function downloads all-MiniLM-L6-v2 as onnx.tar.gz from chroma-onnx-models.s3.amazonaws.com on first use into ~/.cache/chroma/onnx_models/all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (sha256-verified) and requires the onnxruntime package; the docs say model files are downloaded automatically.
     -> An air-gapped first run fails unless the model is pre-seeded. Ship a 'fetch models' step that runs while online (or an optional wheel/extra containing the ONNX files) and point Chroma at an explicit model path under %LOCALAPPDATA%; verify the sha256 locally.
  16. [high] Chroma's chunking study (text-embedding-3-large, 5 retrieved chunks): RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter 400/0 recall 89.5 vs 200/0 recall 88.1 with precision roughly doubled (3.6 -> 7.0) and IoU 3.6 -> 6.9; 800/400 (OpenAI file-search default) had the worst efficiency. Specifically for all-MiniLM-L6-v2, 250-token chunks with 125-token overlap reached recall 82.4 vs 77.1 with no overlap. Anthropic recommends chunks of 'no more than a few hundred tokens' and found top-20 retrieval best. LlamaIndex's '1024 is best' result used OpenAI embeddings and GPT-3.5 answer quality, not a 256-token encoder.
     -> Default chunker: ~200-250 tokens, ~100-token (40-50%) overlap for MiniLM, recursive split on paragraph/sentence boundaries; chunk body and each attachment separately, prefix every chunk with 'From / Date / Subject / Attachment name' context (cheap contextual retrieval); retrieve 20+ chunks before grouping.
  17. [high] Parent-document retrieval (LangChain ParentDocumentRetriever): small chunks embed accurately but lose context, so index child chunks and return the parent they came from; reference example uses child chunk_size=400 and parent chunk_size=2000 with the parent id stored in child metadata and parents kept in a separate docstore.
     -> Vector store holds chunks with metadata {email_key, part ('body'|'att:<name>'), chunk_idx, sender, sent_ts, thread_id, project_id}; after a chunk query, group by email_key, score = max or RRF over its chunks, then load the parent email record (and matching snippet) from SQLite. No LangChain dependency needed.
  18. [high] Chroma where filters support $eq (implicit), $ne, $gt/$gte/$lt/$lte, $in/$nin, $and/$or (lists of filters), metadata scalars str/int/float/bool and homogeneous arrays with $contains/$not_contains; nested arrays, empty arrays and None are not allowed; where_document supports $contains, $not_contains, $regex, $not_regex (case-sensitive) and combines with where in the same query.
     -> Store sent_ts as int epoch for {$gte,$lte}; sender as lower-cased address string for equality; recipients as an array so {'recipients': {'$contains': 'x@y'}} works; omit keys instead of writing None. Build the UI filter model (sender list OR, date range AND) directly as {'$and': [...{'$or': [...]}]}.
  19. [high] SQLite in python.org Windows builds is compiled with SQLITE_ENABLE_FTS5 (PCbuild/sqlite3.vcxproj PreprocessorDefinitions); verified locally on Python 3.14.0 / SQLite 3.50.4 (compile_options contains ENABLE_FTS5). FTS5 provides a built-in bm25() ranking function (ORDER BY rank), unicode61 tokenizer with tokenchars (tested: tokenchars '-_#@.' keeps 'INV-2024-00017' and 'jane.doe@example.com' as single tokens), and a trigram tokenizer (SQLite >=3.34.0, 2020-12-01) giving case-insensitive substring matching for terms >=3 chars (tested: '"2024-000"' matched). Query strings containing punctuation must be double-quoted; external-content tables keep the index in sync via triggers.
     -> Put the manifest, parent email records, chunk text and an FTS5 external-content index in one WAL-mode SQLite file; two FTS tables (unicode61+tokenchars for ranked BM25, trigram for partial IDs). At startup run PRAGMA compile_options and fall back to LIKE if FTS5 is missing (conda/embedded Pythons). Always pass user text as a quoted phrase or sanitized token list to avoid FTS syntax errors.
  20. [high] Lexical search is needed for exact identifiers: Anthropic's contextual-retrieval write-up gives the 'Error code TS-999' example that embeddings miss, reports embeddings+BM25 outperform embeddings alone, and uses rank fusion; BEIR (18 datasets) finds BM25 'a robust baseline' while dense retrievers 'often underperform' out of domain. RRF score = sum 1/(k + rank) with k=60 (Elastic default, Cormack et al. SIGIR 2009) and needs no score normalization.
     -> Hybrid on by default: run FTS5 BM25 (top 50) and Chroma kNN (top 50) with the same metadata filter, fuse with RRF k=60 at chunk level, then group to parent emails. Exact-token hits (invoice numbers, names) surface even when the embedding misses them.
  21. [medium] chromadb 1.5.9 (2026-05-05, cp39-abi3 win_amd64 wheel 23.5 MB) exports Search, Knn, Rrf, Schema, SparseVectorIndexConfig and Bm25EmbeddingFunction / ChromaBm25EmbeddingFunction (snowballstemmer-based, 'runs locally and does not require any external API keys'), but the Search API / sparse-index docs live under /cloud/, use CloudClient in every example, and nowhere state that sparse indexes work with PersistentClient; release notes do not mention it either. Chroma telemetry is on by default and is disabled via Settings(anonymized_telemetry=False) or env ANONYMIZED_TELEMETRY=False.
     -> Do not build on Chroma's native hybrid search for an offline PersistentClient until a 10-minute spike proves SparseVectorIndexConfig works locally; SQLite FTS5 + Python RRF has zero extra dependencies. Always construct the client with anonymized_telemetry=False and set ANONYMIZED_TELEMETRY=False in the launcher.
  22. [high] openpyxl 3.1.5 (2024-06-28, pure Python 251 KB wheel): assigning a str to cell.hyperlink wraps it in Hyperlink(ref=cell, target=str) and sets the cell value to the link text if empty; built-in named styles 'Hyperlink' and 'Followed Hyperlink' exist (cell.style = 'Hyperlink'); Font(bold=True) and Alignment(wrap_text=True) are the documented primitives; openpyxl has no true autosize so widths are set via ws.column_dimensions[letter].width. pandas ExcelWriter defaults to xlsxwriter when installed, so pass engine='openpyxl' explicitly; writer.sheets maps sheet name to the worksheet for post-formatting.
     -> Export pattern: df.to_excel(writer, index=False) -> ws = writer.sheets[name] -> bold header row, freeze_panes='A2', auto_filter, wrap_text on preview column, width = min(60, max len) per column, hyperlink + style 'Hyperlink' on the path column, plus a plain-text path column as a copyable fallback. Do not pull pandas in only for export: a plain openpyxl writer avoids an ~11 MB dependency.
  23. [high] Windows path -> file URI: pathlib.Path(p).as_uri() yields file:///C:/Users/Jane%20Doe/...%C3%BC...%25.msg (percent-encodes spaces, non-ASCII, '#', '%', '&', ':' in names) and for UNC paths file://fileserver/share/Projects/Q1%20Report.msg, identical to 'file:' + urllib.request.pathname2url(p) (verified on Python 3.14). PurePath.as_uri() is deprecated in 3.14 (removal 3.19) and mangles \\?\ long-path prefixes, whereas Path.as_uri() strips them.
     -> Use pathlib.Path(os.path.abspath(p)).as_uri() (never string concatenation). Percent-encoding '#' matters: an unencoded '#' in a subject-derived filename is parsed by Excel as a sheet-location separator and breaks the link.
  24. [low] Excel limits: 32,767 characters per cell, 65,530 hyperlinks per worksheet, 255-character maximum column width. Hyperlink target length limits are only forum-documented and conflicting: several threads report the HYPERLINK() formula failing beyond ~255 characters while cell-level hyperlinks allow ~2,083, and one thread reports the reverse. No Microsoft page states a hyperlink length limit.
     -> Truncate body previews to ~1,000 chars; cap export rows below 65,000 or split sheets; keep file:// targets under ~250 characters where possible (archive root near drive root) and test one export with a deliberately long UNC path on the target Office build before release.
  25. [high] Clicking a file:// hyperlink in Office to a non-Office file type raises the dialog 'Opening "path". Hyperlinks can be harmful to your computer and data... Do you want to continue?' regardless of Trust Center level. It can be disabled by DWORD DisableHyperlinkWarning=1 under HKEY_CURRENT_USER\Software\Microsoft\Office\16.0\Common\Security (user hive, no admin), but group policy may override it, the per-type EditFlags method requires HKEY_CLASSES_ROOT, and the warning still appears in Protected View.
     -> Expect one confirmation dialog per click on corporate machines. Document the HKCU tweak as an optional, user-initiated 'eMailSuperSearch doctor --fix-office-hyperlink-warning' action with consent; never modify the registry silently from a library. Provide an in-UI 'Open in Outlook' / 'Reveal in Explorer' button (os.startfile) as the primary path so Excel links are secondary.
  26. [medium] Protected View is triggered by file origin (downloaded from the Internet, Outlook attachment from an unsafe sender, unsafe locations such as Temporary Internet Files, File Block, failed validation), not by content; a workbook written locally by the user's own process has none of these triggers. Forum reports (no official doc found) say hyperlinks are inert in Protected View until 'Enable Editing'.
     -> Write exports to a folder under the user profile (e.g. %USERPROFILE%\Documents\eMailSuperSearch\exports), not Downloads/Temp; warn in the UI that if the .xlsx is emailed to colleagues their links will be blocked until Enable Editing and will only resolve if they can reach the same path (UNC).
  27. [medium] The new Outlook for Windows gained .msg/.eml/.oft opening in March 2024 (MC713893) but requires a qualifying Microsoft 365 subscription, had a 14 MB .msg size limit and required an active Internet connection at launch; classic Outlook opens any .msg. Which app a file:// .msg hyperlink launches depends on the Windows default-app association for .msg.
     -> Do not assume 'click opens in Outlook' works on every laptop; detect the .msg handler via HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Explorer\FileExts\.msg\UserChoice at startup and surface a diagnostic. Offer 'Reveal in Explorer' as a handler-independent fallback.
  28. [high] Alternatives are weak: msg_parser 1.2.0 last released 2019-12-14 (last commit 2022-03-19); independentsoft.msg 1.6 is proprietary, sdist-only, last released 2019-11-14; Aspose.Email-for-Python-via-NET 26.10 (2026-10-16) is a 58 MB proprietary win_amd64 wheel requiring Python <3.14. For PST/OST, libpff-python 20260926 ships win_amd64 wheels for cp310-cp315 (367 KB), import name pypff, status 'alpha', LGPL-3.0-or-later; the PyPI project literally named 'pypff' is an unrelated PANOSETI astronomy package.
     -> extract-msg is the only viable OSS .msg parser. PST/OST ingestion is pip-only feasible as an optional extra 'emailsupersearch[pst]' -> libpff-python (alpha; pin exact date-version and fence behind a feature flag); never list 'pypff' as a dependency.


### Verifier
recommendation_holds=True

**Revised recommendation:** Keep the researcher's architecture (extract-msg behind an adapter, SQLite manifest + reconciliation scan with watchdog only as a debounced trigger, 200-250-token child chunks in Chroma plus FTS5 external-content index, RRF k=60 fused and grouped to parent emails, openpyxl export with Path.as_uri() links and an os.startfile 'Open in Outlook' primary action) with these amendments. (1) Licensing: extract-msg is GPL-3.0; either accept GPL-compatible licensing for the PyPI package, isolate it as an optional extra, or adopt MIT python-oxmsg as the default parser with extract-msg as an opt-in extra for RTF-only bodies and legacy encodings. (2) Manifest: use os.scandir (size, mtime_ns) as the cheap change key, call os.stat for st_ino only on new/changed rows, treat file_id as a rename hint not an identity (os.replace changes it), and hash lazily; keep two-phase deletes and the scan_id sweep. (3) Add .eml ingestion (stdlib email) because new Outlook drag-drop produces .eml, not .msg, and new Outlook cannot drag-export .msg at all. (4) Drop Chroma native hybrid search from the alternatives list: Chroma's docs state the Search API is Cloud-only with local support only 'in a future release'. (5) Chunk overlap: the MiniLM overlap gain (82.4 vs 77.1) is TokenTextSplitter-only; RecursiveCharacterTextSplitter showed no recall gain and lower precision, so use a small overlap (about 10-20%) and benchmark rather than 50%. (6) FTS5: unicode61 tokenchars '-_#@.' keeps identifiers whole but then the bare token '2024' no longer matches inside 'INV-2024-00017'; either index a second plain-unicode61 column or rely on the trigram table for substring hits. (7) Export: Excel's hyperlink warning is documented for 'unsafe' target types and is forum-reported for local file links, but not confirmed by Microsoft for .msg; test on the target Office build, keep the plain-path column, consider XlsxWriter if no .xlsx reading is needed (default hyperlink style, autofit, URL-length guard), and set engine='openpyxl' explicitly if pandas is used. (8) Offline: pre-seed Chroma's all-MiniLM-L6-v2 ONNX archive into the user profile (it otherwise downloads from S3 on first use) and set anonymized_telemetry=False.

### Missed Options

  - python-oxmsg 0.0.2 (PyPI 2025-02-03, MIT, pure Python py3-none-any 31 KB, >=3.9, deps click/olefile/typing_extensions; author scanny of python-docx): Message.body, html_body, message_headers, recipients, sender, sent_date, subject, attachments (file_bytes/mime_type/last_modified) and raw .properties access. Permissively licensed drop-in for the parsing layer, which matters because extract-msg is GPL-3.0 and importing it from a PyPI library carries copyleft obligations the researcher never addressed. Gaps: 0.0.x maturity, no RTF de-encapsulation, no documented non-Unicode/embedded-msg handling. Sources: https://pypi.org/pypi/python-oxmsg/json, https://scanny.github.io/python-oxmsg/message/
  - pywin32 312 (PyPI 2026-06-04, cp310-cp313 win_amd64 wheels ~6.9 MB, PSF licence, no admin needed) driving the already-installed classic Outlook via COM: Namespace.OpenSharedItem(path) returns a MailItem for any .msg with ConversationIndex/ConversationTopic/PropertyAccessor, Outlook's own encoding/RTF handling, and Display() for 'Open in Outlook'. Caveats: classic Outlook only (new Outlook has no COM object model), slower than pure parsing, Outlook security prompts for address access. Useful as an optional 'outlook' extra or fallback parser. Sources: https://pypi.org/pypi/pywin32/json, https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/office/vba/api/outlook.namespace.openshareditem
  - .eml ingestion via the stdlib email package: a Microsoft moderator confirms the new Outlook for Windows saves drag-dropped messages as .eml, not .msg, so a '.msg folder mirror' silently misses mail from users on new Outlook. Zero-dependency email.message_from_bytes/BytesParser(policy=default) covers it. Source: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/answers/questions/5915469/how-can-i-drag-and-drop-a-msg-from-the-new-outlook
  - XlsxWriter 3.3.0 (PyPI 2026-10-22, pure Python py3-none-any 180 KB): write_url() applies Excel's default hyperlink style automatically, enforces a configurable max_url_length (2,079 default, 255 for pre-2015 Excel), supports network paths, and has worksheet.autofit(); write-only, so it fits an export-only path and is what pandas picks by default. Source: https://pypi.org/pypi/xlsxwriter/json, https://xlsxwriter.readthedocs.io/worksheet.html
  - tantivy 0.27.0 (PyPI 2026-10-22, cp311-abi3 win_amd64 wheel 4.3 MB, Rust Lucene-style engine with BM25, incremental add/delete, persistent index, phrase and fuzzy queries) as a BM25 alternative to FTS5 when Python >=3.11 is acceptable. Source: https://pypi.org/pypi/tantivy/json
  - sqlite-vec 0.1.9 (PyPI 2026-03-31, py3-none-win_amd64 wheel 293 KB, MIT/Apache) to keep vectors in the same SQLite file as the FTS5 index and the manifest, removing Chroma's 23.5 MB wheel, onnxruntime/opentelemetry/grpc/kubernetes dependency tree and telemetry from the required install (embedding then via a separately chosen ONNX runtime). Source: https://pypi.org/pypi/sqlite-vec/json
  - Design detail missed in the manifest plan: on Windows os.scandir() DirEntry.stat() returns st_ino/st_dev/st_nlink as zero (os.rst), so file_id needs a per-file os.stat() (an extra open on SMB); and os.replace()-style atomic saves change st_ino (local test), so (path, size, mtime_ns) from scandir should be the fast key and file_id only a rename hint.



---

## Area: llm-engine


### Summary

The PRD's engine choice is the weakest part of the stack: the gpt4all PyPI package is frozen at 2.8.2 (uploaded 2024-08-14), the GitHub repo's last push was 2025-05-27 (a CI bump), the last app release was v3.10.0 (2025-02-25), and open issues #3558/#3605/#3690/#3708 asking "is this dead?" have had no maintainer reply for 18 months; its device="amd" path is the Kompute/Vulkan backend that only GPU-accelerates Q4_0/Q4_1 GGUFs and its Windows wheel silently needs msvcp140/vcruntime140 DLLs. The only pip-installable, no-compiler, no-admin engine with current releases and AMD-iGPU acceleration is llama-cpp-python (0.3.36, 2026-10-01) via abetlen's prebuilt py3-none-win_amd64 Vulkan or CPU wheels on a GitHub-Pages extra index (not PyPI itself); llama.cpp Vulkan on the Ryzen 8000-series iGPU benchmarks at ~20 t/s tg128 for 7B Q4_0 and ~11-12 t/s for Llama-3 8B Q5_K_M. The ONNX Runtime GenAI route is in flux: the DirectML pip package stopped at 0.14.1 (June 2026) and its dependency onnxruntime-directml>=1.26 does not exist on PyPI (latest 1.24.4), DirectML is officially "sustained engineering/legacy", and the pip-only successor is the experimental WebGPU plugin EP (onnxruntime-ep-webgpu 0.4.0) or the WinML wheel that downloads EPs on demand on Windows 11 24H2. Lemonade is now a C++ server shipped as an MSI (no PyPI package), the Hawk Point XDNA1 NPU has no LLM support from any engine, MLC needs conda, ctransformers is dead (2023). On memory, Llama 3 8B Q4_0/Q4_K_M (4.66/4.92 GB) plus a 1.0 GiB f16 KV cache at 8k context lands at ~5.7-6.0 GB before compute buffers, i.e. at or over the 6 GB cap; Llama-3.2-3B, Qwen2.5-3B, Qwen3-4B and Phi-4-mini at Q4_K_M all fit in 2.5-3.7 GB. Weights cannot ship on PyPI (100 MiB default/1 GiB hard per-file cap); distribute GGUFs from Hugging Face via huggingface_hub with HF_HUB_OFFLINE afterwards, plus a sideload/import path with sha256 verification. ONNX Runtime and ONNX Runtime GenAI (>=0.15.0) and huggingface_hub all phone home by default and must be disabled by env var.


### Recommended

Replace gpt4all with llama-cpp-python 0.3.36 using abetlen's prebuilt py3-none-win_amd64 Vulkan wheel (CPU wheel as the opt-out/fallback), loading plain GGUF weights fetched by huggingface_hub into %LOCALAPPDATA% with HF_HUB_OFFLINE/telemetry env vars set and sha256-verified sideload support. It is the only candidate that is actively released (Oct 2026), needs no compiler, admin, installer or OS-version gate, accelerates on the Radeon 760M via Vulkan (~10-12 t/s on 8B Q4, ~20 t/s on 3-4B), has no telemetry to disable, and uses one artifact format across CPU and GPU. Default the shipped model to a 3-4B Q4_K_M GGUF (Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 or Llama-3.2-3B; ~2.5-3.7 GB with 8k f16 KV) and offer Llama-3.1-8B Q4_K_M as an optional download with a 4k-context or q8 KV-cache default, because 8B at 8k is ~5.7-6.0 GB and exceeds the 6 GB cap. Resolve the two packaging gaps up front: the wheel lives on a GitHub-Pages extra index (vendor the DLLs into your own PyPI wheel or install it on first run), and the MSVC runtime must be detected or shipped app-locally since a standard user cannot run vc_redist.


### Alternatives

  - onnxruntime + onnxruntime-ep-webgpu + onnxruntime-genai (pure PyPI, cp311-314, any DX12 GPU via Dawn) — modern Microsoft stack with int4 ONNX models, but WebGPU EP is experimental/unverified on Radeon 760M, needs per-EP ONNX artifacts (few Qwen2.5/Llama 3.x GenAI builds exist for WebGPU), and telemetry is on by default (ORT_DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1 mandatory).
  - onnxruntime-genai-winml (PyPI, cp311-314) — EPs auto-managed by Windows ML, but requires Windows 11 24H2 for downloaded EPs, downloads EPs over the network, AMD GPU EP (MIGraphX) is 'not supported for GenAI', and VitisAI needs admin driver installs; conflicts with offline/no-admin constraints.
  - Keep gpt4all 2.8.2 (PRD) — zero engineering change and MD5/resume downloader built in, but frozen since Aug 2024 with an unresponsive maintainer, Vulkan limited to Q4_0/Q4_1, weak AMD performance reports, undisclosed msvcp140 dependency, and a CDN with no owner.
  - llama-cpp-python CPU-only wheel (no Vulkan) — simplest and most robust (no GPU driver variance), but ~2x slower than Vulkan on this iGPU; sensible as the default when the user opts out of the GPU component.
  - Lemonade Server (AMD) — best AMD tuning and zero telemetry, but now a C++ MSI installer rather than a pip package, so it violates the pip-only constraint; the Hawk Point NPU is unsupported regardless.
  - transformers + torch CPU — pure PyPI and universal, but 124 MB torch wheel, bf16 weights (6.4 GB for 3B) and slow CPU decoding make it unsuitable for 16 GB UMA and the 6 GB cap.


### Install Footprint

Recommended (llama-cpp-python route): llama_cpp_python-0.3.36-py3-none-win_amd64 Vulkan wheel 42.1 MB (CPU wheel from the same index, size not measured here); huggingface_hub 0.9 MB + hf_xet 3.8 MB; default model Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 Q4_K_M 2.5 GB or Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct Q4_K_M 2.02 GB (Qwen2.5-3B Q4_K_M 2.1 GB; Phi-4-mini Q4_K_M 2.49 GB); optional Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct Q4_K_M 4.92 GB (Q4_0 4.66 GB). PRD option (gpt4all): gpt4all-2.8.2-py3-none-win_amd64.whl 119.6 MB + Meta-Llama-3-8B-Instruct.Q4_0.gguf 4.66 GB from gpt4all.io. ONNX alternative: onnxruntime 1.31.0 win_amd64 14.9 MB + onnxruntime-ep-webgpu 0.4.0 13.1 MB + onnxruntime-genai 0.18.0 4.3 MB (or onnxruntime-genai-winml 25.6 MB) + model e.g. Phi-4-mini gpu-int4 3.41 GB or Qwen3-4B webgpu-int4 3.07 GB. Not applicable: torch 2.14.1 CPU wheel 124.1 MB.


### Open Questions For Owner

  - GPU wheel delivery: is it acceptable to tell users to add --extra-index-url https://abetlen.github.io/llama-cpp-python/whl/vulkan (not PyPI), or should the library vendor the ~42 MB llama.cpp DLLs in its own PyPI wheel / download the wheel on first run? Pure 'pip install yourlib' alone cannot reach that index.
  - Default model quality vs budget: accept a 3-4B default (Qwen3-4B / Llama-3.2-3B, ~3 GB, ~20 t/s) with 8B as an optional download, or insist on Llama-3 8B and relax either the 6 GB cap or the 8k context (4k context or q8 KV cache)?
  - Can corporate laptops reach huggingface.co and its CDN? If HF is blocked by proxy policy, the sideload/import-from-file-share path becomes the primary distribution channel and needs IT involvement for the first copy.
  - MSVC runtime policy: may the library ship msvcp140.dll/vcruntime140.dll app-locally (Microsoft redistribution licence requires the developer hold a Visual Studio licence; Microsoft 'doesn't recommend' app-local for servicing), or should it only detect and report the missing runtime and rely on IT images already having VC++ 2015-2022 installed?
  - Is GPU acceleration a hard requirement or an opt-in extra? If CPU-only is acceptable for the baseline, the modular install becomes simpler (one CPU wheel, no Vulkan driver variance) and the GPU component can be the opt-in.
  - Should the library pin to Python 3.11-3.13 (what all candidate wheels cover) and which Python provisioning route (python.org per-user installer, winget, Store) will IT permit on the laptops?
  - Do you want a second ONNX Runtime GenAI (WebGPU) backend prototyped as a hedge against llama-cpp-python wheel availability, given its on-by-default telemetry and experimental EP status?


### Claims

  1. [high] PyPI 'gpt4all' latest is 2.8.2, uploaded 2024-08-14; the Windows artifact is a single py3-none-win_amd64 wheel (119.6 MB) that pip accepts on any Python 3 (cp311/312/313), but no release has shipped in over two years.
     -> Any bug (new GGUF metadata, new Llama/Qwen architectures, Vulkan driver changes) cannot be fixed upstream; treat gpt4all as a dead dependency for a product launching in 2026-27.
  2. [high] The nomic-ai/gpt4all repo is not archived but its last push was 2025-05-27 (a CI orb bump), last code commit 2025-02-25, last release v3.10.0 on 2025-02-25; issues #3558 'Development stopped?' (Apr 2025, 49 comments), #3605, #3690 and #3708 'GPT4All is no longer maintained' have zero MEMBER/COLLABORATOR replies. No official Nomic pause/EOL announcement was found; the project is effectively unmaintained.
     -> Do not build the RAG/triple-extraction layer on the gpt4all Python API; abstract the engine behind an interface so the backend can be swapped.
  3. [high] In gpt4all's Python binding, device="amd" means 'best GPU from this vendor via the Kompute backend' (Nomic's Vulkan fork); Nomic Vulkan GPU-accelerates only Q4_0 and Q4_1 GGUF quantizations, which is why the PRD is forced onto Q4_0.
     -> Q4_K_M (better quality per byte) and K-quant/IQ models would silently fall back to CPU under gpt4all; llama.cpp's Vulkan backend has no such restriction.
  4. [high] gpt4all's Windows wheel needs msvcp140.dll, vcruntime140.dll and vcruntime140_1.dll; Nomic added an import-time warning pointing to vc_redist.x64.exe in commit 46314dc7 (2024-08-30), which post-dates the 2.8.2 release, so 2.8.2 fails with an opaque DLL error when the runtime is missing.
     -> Any native engine must either bundle the MSVC runtime app-locally or probe for it at startup and give an actionable message; a standard user cannot run vc_redist.x64.exe (UAC elevation required).
  5. [medium] Reported GPT4All performance on AMD GPUs is poor and inconsistent: users report 4-5 t/s with no gain over CPU after selecting the AMD device, and ~12 t/s on an 8B Q4_0 (only ~50% over CPU) on a 16 GB discrete card; a Nomic maintainer acknowledged the Nomic Vulkan kernels are less optimized than upstream llama.cpp. No 760M/780M-specific gpt4all number was found.
     -> Expect gpt4all on the 760M to deliver little or no speedup over CPU; the PRD's tok/s assumptions should be re-baselined on llama.cpp Vulkan.
  6. [medium] Upstream llama.cpp Vulkan on the 'AMD Ryzen 8000 Series' iGPU (Hawk Point, Radeon 780M, 12 CU) measures pp512 267-311 t/s and tg128 19.8-21.3 t/s on Llama-2 7B Q4_0 (community llama-bench table); a 780M running Llama 3 8B Q5_K_M fully offloaded via Vulkan reports ~11-12 t/s generation (Linux RADV, March 2026). A llama.cpp maintainer states Vulkan is faster than HIP on this architecture and AMD does not support gfx1103 under ROCm.
     -> Plan for ~10-12 t/s on 8B Q4 and ~20 t/s on 3-4B Q4 on the 760M (8 CU; tg is bandwidth-bound so similar to 780M, prompt processing lower); batch triple extraction during ingestion will be prompt-processing-bound at a few hundred tok/s.
  7. [high] llama.cpp changed iGPU defaults in 2026: --mmap/--no-mmap was replaced by --load-mode (PR #26934, Aug 2026) and lazy tensor loading is disabled by default on iGPUs (PR #28326, Sep 2026) because mmap on iGPUs caused a duplicate model copy in unified memory and large performance loss.
     -> Pin llama-cpp-python >= 0.3.35/0.3.36 so the iGPU-friendly defaults apply; on 16 GB UMA, avoid any configuration that holds two copies of the weights.
  8. [high] PyPI 'llama-cpp-python' latest is 0.3.36 (2026-10-01) but PyPI carries only an sdist (building needs Visual Studio/MinGW + CMake). Prebuilt Windows wheels live on abetlen's GitHub-Pages index: --extra-index-url https://abetlen.github.io/llama-cpp-python/whl/cpu and .../whl/vulkan, each publishing llama_cpp_python-0.3.36-py3-none-win_amd64.whl (Vulkan wheel 42.1 MB) for every release 0.3.29-0.3.36; the py3-none tag works on cp311/312/313 because the binding is ctypes-based.
     -> Pure-PyPI 'pip install yourlib' cannot pull these wheels (extras cannot name an index). Options: document --extra-index-url for the [llm] extra; vendor the 42 MB llama.cpp DLLs inside your own PyPI wheel (under the 100 MiB cap); or have the library pip-install the wheel from that index on first run.
  9. [medium] The abetlen Windows Vulkan wheel is built on windows-2022 with MSVC (ilammy/msvc-dev-cmd, Ninja, -DGGML_VULKAN=on, Vulkan SDK 1.4.341.0) via plain 'python -m build --wheel' with no delvewheel/repair step, so the MSVC runtime DLLs (msvcp140/vcruntime140) are almost certainly not bundled and must exist on the target machine; the Vulkan loader itself is 'delivered with the vendor's driver package', so no Vulkan SDK is needed at runtime.
     -> Verify on a clean standard-user VM; if msvcp140.dll is missing, ship it app-local next to llama.dll (Microsoft permits application-local deployment but 'doesn't recommend' it for servicing) or detect and instruct, since vc_redist needs admin.
  10. [high] Installing Python itself without admin is supported: the python.org installer's 'Install Now' says 'You will not need to be an administrator (unless a system update for the C Runtime Library is required...)' and installs into the user directory; the Python install manager runtimes are 'all per-user only'; winget/Microsoft Store are alternatives.
     -> Document a per-user Python 3.11-3.13 install as the single prerequisite; the C-runtime caveat in Python's own docs reinforces the MSVC-runtime risk above.
  11. [high] PyPI 'onnxruntime-genai' latest is 0.18.0 (2026-10-14) with cp311/cp312/cp313/cp314 win_amd64 wheels (4.3 MB) depending on onnxruntime>=1.31.0 (onnxruntime 1.31.0, 2026-10-09, win_amd64 14.9 MB). The DirectML variant 'onnxruntime-genai-directml' is stuck at 0.14.1 (2026-06-02), GitHub release assets dropped the '-dml' zips from v0.15.0 onward, and 0.14.1 requires onnxruntime-directml>=1.26.0 while PyPI's latest onnxruntime-directml is 1.24.4 (2026-03-17), so the pip DirectML path is unresolvable from PyPI today even though the docs still say 'pip install onnxruntime-genai-directml'.
     -> Do not design around onnxruntime-genai-directml; if ONNX is chosen, target the WebGPU plugin EP or WinML, and pin exact versions because the packaging is mid-transition.
  12. [high] Microsoft states 'DirectML is in sustained engineering... new feature development has moved to Windows ML'; the Windows ML EP list marks DirectML '(legacy)'. DirectML still runs on 'all DirectX 12-compatible hardware' (the Radeon 760M qualifies).
     -> DirectML is a maintenance-only dead end for new GenAI work; it should not be the primary GPU path for a product with a multi-year life.
  13. [medium] The pip-only GPU path for ONNX Runtime on Windows is the WebGPU plugin EP: 'pip install onnxruntime onnxruntime-ep-webgpu' (onnxruntime-ep-webgpu 0.4.0, 2026-09-22, py3-none-win_amd64 13.1 MB, Python>=3.11), using Dawn over D3D12 or Vulkan on 'any GPU supported by the platform's native graphics API'; onnxruntime-genai exposes register_execution_provider_library(), and onnx-community publishes GenAI-format WebGPU int4 builds (e.g. Qwen3-4B-ONNX onnxruntime/webgpu/webgpu-int4-kld-block-32, 3.07 GB). Microsoft labels the WebGPU EP 'experimental'.
     -> Viable as a second backend (no admin, no extra index), but unproven on Radeon 760M and experimental; prototype before committing.
  14. [medium] 'onnxruntime-genai-winml' 0.18.0 (cp311-cp314 win_amd64, 25.6 MB) exists on PyPI; it discovers EPs through the Windows ML ExecutionProviderCatalog, which downloads vendor EPs on demand only on Windows 11 24H2+ and includes only CPU and DirectML (legacy) out of the box. For AMD hardware the catalog offers MIGraphX ('not supported for GenAI scenarios today') and VitisAI (NPU; requires specific Adrenalin/NPU driver versions, i.e. admin driver installs).
     -> WinML adds a network-dependent EP download and OS-version gate that conflicts with the offline/standard-user constraints; not recommended as primary.
  15. [high] Ready-made ONNX int4 GenAI-format models are uneven: microsoft/Phi-4-mini-instruct-onnx has cpu int4 (4.86 GB) and gpu int4 (3.41 GB); onnx-community/Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct-GENAI-ONNX has cpu int4 (3.65 GB) and cuda int4 (2.63 GB) but no DML/WebGPU folder; onnx-community/Qwen3-4B-ONNX has cpu/cuda/webgpu GenAI folders; no onnx-community GenAI build exists for Qwen2.5-3B/7B (only AMD Ryzen-AI NPU/hybrid and individual uploads); Llama-3.1-8B int4 ONNX exists only from nvidia (CUDA) and amd (Ryzen AI). The GenAI model builder supports Llama, Phi, Qwen, Gemma, Mistral etc., so a vendor can convert and host its own.
     -> Choosing ONNX means the project must build and host its own int4 ONNX artifacts per EP (cpu/webgpu), whereas GGUF has one artifact per model usable on CPU and Vulkan alike.
  16. [high] AMD Lemonade is no longer pip-installable: 'lemonade-sdk' returns 404 on pypi.org and is absent from pypi.amd.com/simple; the current Lemonade is a C++ server distributed as a Windows MSI (CMake 3.28/VS2022 to build from source), with 'Transparent, zero telemetry'. AMD's Ryzen AI docs still show the old 'pip install lemonade-sdk[dev,oga-ryzenai] --extra-index-url=https://pypi.amd.com/simple' inside conda.
     -> Lemonade violates constraint 1 (installer) and cannot be a pip dependency; its only value is as a reference that AMD itself uses llama.cpp+Vulkan for Ryzen 7000/8000 iGPUs.
  17. [high] The 8640HS NPU (XDNA1, Hawk Point) is unsupported for LLMs: AMD's Ryzen AI LLM table lists 'Ryzen AI 7000/8000: GPU/CPU only' (NPU-only and hybrid are Ryzen AI 300 STX/KRK only); Lemonade's FAQ says 'No inference engine providers have plans to support NPUs prior to Ryzen AI 300-series'; Ryzen AI Software's NPU driver install requires 'Open a terminal in administrator mode', and Lemonade's FLM backend lists only XDNA2.
     -> Drop the NPU from the design entirely; the iGPU (Vulkan) and CPU are the only accelerators available on this laptop.
  18. [medium] MLC-LLM is not on PyPI (only a 2022 placeholder 'mlc-ai' 0.1); wheels come from https://mlc.ai/wheels as nightly packages and the Windows CPU+Vulkan path requires conda-forge packages (clang, libvulkan-loader, git-lfs, zstd) for JIT compilation. ctransformers' last release is 0.2.27 (2023-09-10). PyTorch 2.14.1 ships a 124 MB CPU win_amd64 wheel and transformers 5.20.2 is pure-Python, but bf16 3B weights are ~6.4 GB and CPU fp32/bf16 decoding is far slower than 4-bit llama.cpp (not benchmarked here).
     -> Exclude MLC (conda/JIT), ctransformers (dead) and transformers+torch (memory/speed, 124 MB+ install) from the modular install matrix.
  19. [high] Weight footprints (GGUF file size + f16 KV cache at 8192 tokens): Llama 3/3.1 8B Q4_0 4.66 GB or Q4_K_M 4.92 GB + 1.00 GiB KV (32 layers x 8 KV heads x 128) = ~5.7-6.0 GB before compute buffers; Llama-3.2-3B Q4_K_M 2.02 GB + 0.88 GiB = ~3.0 GB; Qwen2.5-3B Q4_K_M 2.1 GB + 0.28 GiB (2 KV heads) = ~2.5 GB; Qwen2.5-7B Q4_K_M 4.68 GB + 0.44 GiB = ~5.2 GB; Phi-4-mini Q4_K_M 2.49 GB + 1.00 GiB = ~3.6 GB; Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 Q4_K_M 2.5 GB + 1.12 GiB = ~3.7 GB.
     -> An 8B model at 8k context does not fit the 6 GB budget; either default to a 3-4B model, cap 8B at ~4k context, or quantize the KV cache (q8_0 halves it) and accept ~5.5 GB.
  20. [medium] On AMD APUs under Windows the iGPU can use the BIOS UMA carve-out (typically 512 MB-4 GB) plus the WDDM shared pool (~50% of RAM) dynamically; Vulkan allocates from both, whereas ROCm-style tools query only the dedicated amount. On 16 GB this is ~8 GB GPU-visible, shared with ChromaDB/embeddings/OCR and the browser.
     -> Do not require users to change BIOS VRAM; budget total process memory (LLM + embedder + vector DB) and keep the LLM under ~4 GB by default so ingestion and chat can coexist on 16 GB.
  21. [high] PyPI limits are hard-coded in Warehouse: MAX_FILESIZE = 100 MiB per file, MAX_PROJECT_SIZE = 10 GiB per project, and UPLOAD_LIMIT_CAP = 1 GiB (the admin form caps per-file increases at 1 GiB; total project size has 'no maximum cap' but only by support request after first uploading a release under the limit).
     -> A 2-5 GB GGUF can never be a PyPI file; weights must be fetched post-install (Hugging Face) or sideloaded. Native engine DLLs (42 MB) do fit in a PyPI wheel.
  22. [high] huggingface_hub 2.3.0 (2026-10-23) with hf_xet 1.7.0 (win_amd64 abi3 wheel 3.8 MB) is the standard downloader: hf_hub_download/snapshot_download/`hf download` support local_dir, allow_patterns, dry_run and local_files_only; LFS files are content-addressed by sha256 (ETag) and Xet reconstructs files from chunk hashes keyed by the LFS SHA256; HF_HOME relocates the cache into the user profile; HF_HUB_DISABLE_SYMLINKS avoids Windows symlink issues for non-admin users.
     -> Implement `yourlib download-model <name>` on huggingface_hub with HF_HOME=%LOCALAPPDATA%\<app>\models, HF_HUB_DISABLE_SYMLINKS=1, and record the expected sha256 in a signed manifest; add `import-model <path>` for USB/file-share sideload that verifies the same hash.
  23. [high] gpt4all's own model catalog (gpt4all.io/models/models3.json) now 301-redirects to raw.githubusercontent.com and most of its 32 entries already point to Hugging Face URLs; the gpt4all.io CDN still served Meta-Llama-3-8B-Instruct.Q4_0.gguf (HTTP 200, 4,661,724,384 bytes, md5 c87ad09e...) on 2026-10-23. The binding resumes with HTTP Range requests into a .part file and verifies MD5 and size.
     -> The gpt4all CDN has no maintainer and could vanish; do not depend on it. Its resume+hash pattern is worth copying, but use sha256 rather than MD5.
  24. [high] ONNX Runtime GenAI >= 0.15.0 (2026-07-30) added 'cross-platform 1DS telemetry to the compiled library (opt-out)'; its Privacy.md says 'Telemetry is turned ON by default in official packages' and sends trace events 'to Microsoft's telemetry backend over HTTPS'; full opt-out requires ORT_DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1 before initialization (API suppression still emits a process-info event).
     -> If any ONNX path is used, the library must set ORT_DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1 in-process before importing onnxruntime/onnxruntime_genai and document it for the zero-telemetry requirement.
  25. [high] ONNX Runtime official builds have telemetry 'turned ON by default', using ETW on Windows components and the 1DS SDK elsewhere; disable with ORT_DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1 before init or onnxruntime.disable_telemetry_events() in Python.
     -> Same env-var gate as above; include a startup self-check that asserts the variable is set.
  26. [high] huggingface_hub sends library usage telemetry by default (send_telemetry; disable with HF_HUB_DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1 or DO_NOT_TRACK=1), the `hf` CLI checks PyPI for updates every 24 h (HF_HUB_DISABLE_UPDATE_CHECK=1), and hf_hub_download makes a HEAD/ETag request even for cached files unless HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1.
     -> Set HF_HUB_DISABLE_TELEMETRY=1, HF_HUB_DISABLE_UPDATE_CHECK=1 always, and HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 whenever not inside the explicit download-model command.
  27. [medium] gpt4all's Python binding contains no telemetry: its only network calls are GET https://gpt4all.io/models/models3.json (list_models when allow_download=True) and model downloads; llama-cpp-python's README/documentation describes no telemetry or update checks; Lemonade advertises 'zero telemetry'.
     -> llama-cpp-python is the only engine candidate with no phone-home surface to disable; with gpt4all, pass allow_download=False in offline mode.


### Verifier
recommendation_holds=True

**Revised recommendation:** The recommendation holds and is strengthened: llama-cpp-python 0.3.36 with abetlen's py3-none-win_amd64 Vulkan wheel (CPU wheel as opt-out) loading plain GGUF via huggingface_hub into %LOCALAPPDATA% remains the only actively released, pip-installable, admin-free, telemetry-free path that accelerates on the Radeon 760M. Two corrections tighten the packaging plan: (1) the MSVC runtime gap is now a verified fact, not an inference - ggml-base.dll, ggml-vulkan.dll and llama.dll in the 0.3.36 Vulkan wheel dynamically import MSVCP140.dll, VCRUNTIME140.dll and VCRUNTIME140_1.dll and the wheel bundles none of them, so ship them app-locally (permitted by Microsoft's redistribution docs) or build a static-CRT wheel, and detect before loading; gpt4all 2.8.2 has the identical dependency with no warning. (2) The abetlen index is not perfectly regular (no 0.3.29 anywhere, no 0.3.31 CPU wheel), so pin exact versions and vendor the DLLs into your own PyPI wheel (42 MB fits under the 100 MiB limit) rather than relying on a first-run extra-index install. Keep a 3-4B Q4_K_M default (Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 ~3.7 GB or Llama-3.2-3B ~3.0 GB at 8k f16 KV; arithmetic verified) and offer Llama-3.1-8B Q4_K_M only with 4k context or q8 KV. Expect ~11-12 t/s on 8B and ~20 t/s on 7B-class Q4_0 from Vulkan on Phoenix/Hawk Point per community numbers (Linux RADV; no Windows 760M figure exists, so benchmark on the target). Use llama.cpp's current defaults (no mmap/lazy loading on iGPUs) and consider the official ggml-org Windows Vulkan zip run as a llama-server subprocess as a fallback engine with faster upstream cadence and CPU-dispatch DLLs. All alternatives remain disqualified for the stated reasons: ONNX GenAI's pip DirectML path is unresolvable and WebGPU EP is experimental with Microsoft telemetry on by default; Windows ML needs 24H2 and on-demand EP downloads with no usable AMD GPU EP for GenAI; Lemonade is an MSI; the XDNA1 NPU is unsupported everywhere; gpt4all is unmaintained since Feb 2025.

### Missed Options

  - Official ggml-org llama.cpp Windows Vulkan binaries (llama-b11840-bin-win-vulkan-x64.zip, 37 MB, released 2026-10-23, new build almost daily) run as a llama-server subprocess behind its OpenAI-compatible HTTP API: same GGUF artifacts, upstream release cadence instead of abetlen's, and it ships per-CPU-level ggml-cpu-*.dll variants (sse42/haswell/alderlake/skylakex/sapphirerapids...) with runtime dispatch, whereas the abetlen wheel is built with GGML_NATIVE=off and a single ggml-cpu.dll. Download-on-first-run with sha256 pinning; CRT dependency likely identical (unverified). https://api.github.com/repos/ggml-org/llama.cpp/releases?per_page=1
  - App-local CRT shipping is explicitly permitted by Microsoft ('It's also possible to directly install the Redistributable DLLs in the application local folder', not recommended for servicing) and distribution is allowed to licensed VS users per the REDIST list, so the library can carry msvcp140/vcruntime140/vcruntime140_1 next to llama_cpp/lib and skip vc_redist entirely; alternatively build your own wheel with -DCMAKE_MSVC_RUNTIME_LIBRARY=MultiThreaded (static CRT) to remove the dependency. Also detect first: most corporate images already have VC++ 2015-2022 x64 via Office/Teams. https://learn.microsoft.com/cpp/windows/redistributing-visual-cpp-files
  - ik-llama-cpp-python 0.1.5 (PyPI, 2026-09-16, cp310-cp313 win_amd64 wheels, 10.4 MB, AVX2 CPU, drop-in llama-cpp-python API) - a true PyPI-hosted CPU fallback needing no extra index, though it is a young single-maintainer project with no Vulkan wheel. https://pypi.org/pypi/ik-llama-cpp-python/json
  - openvino-genai 2026.4.1.0 + openvino 2026.4.1 (pure PyPI, cp310-cp314 win_amd64, ~89 MB total): CPU int4 LLM pipeline that works on AMD CPUs; GPU/NPU plugins are Intel-only so no Radeon 760M acceleration, but it is a Microsoft-telemetry-free, PyPI-only CPU alternative to the abetlen CPU wheel. https://pypi.org/pypi/openvino-genai/json
  - ctranslate2 4.8.3 (PyPI, cp39-cp314 win_amd64, 19.2 MB): CPU int8 Llama/Qwen generation with no extra index; weaker than 4-bit llama.cpp on 16 GB but fully PyPI. https://pypi.org/pypi/ctranslate2/json
  - Vendoring the abetlen Vulkan wheel contents into the project's own PyPI wheel is feasible size-wise: the Vulkan wheel is 42 MB and the CPU wheel smaller, both under Warehouse's 100 MiB per-file default, so no PyPI limit increase is needed for the engine (only model weights must stay off PyPI).



---

## Area: vector-store


### Summary

chromadb 1.5.9 (2026-05-05) is installable on the target: one cp39-abi3 win_amd64 wheel (23.5 MB) covers cp311/cp312/cp313, the Rust core bundles its own SQLite, chroma-hnswlib is no longer a runtime dependency, and since 1.5.4 Chroma collects no product telemetry at all (posthog.py is a no-op, the posthog dependency is gone, the docs say so). But it drags in ~85 packages (~83 MB: kubernetes, grpcio, opentelemetry, uvicorn, typer/rich...) and its DefaultEmbeddingFunction silently downloads an 83 MB tarball from S3 into ~/.cache/chroma on first use, which breaks "nothing but pip" unless you pre-seed that exact path or supply your own embedding function. hnswlib from PyPI is sdist-only (needs MSVC) and must not be used; usearch/voyager/faiss-cpu/sqlite-vec/LanceDB all ship Windows wheels. For 10k-100k emails (~300k chunks of 256-512 tokens) exact brute-force search is sufficient: sqlite-vec (0.3 MB, zero deps) or a numpy memmap answers a 300k x 384 query in roughly 10-30 ms, and a 1M x 128-d sqlite-vec scan is measured at 33 ms; HNSW only pays off past ~500k vectors. Embeddings without torch: onnxruntime (14.9 MB wheel) running an int8 ONNX all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (23 MB) or bge-small-en-v1.5 (34 MB) shipped inside a separate PyPI wheel (PyPI cap is 100 MB/file), with model2vec potion-base-8M (30 MB, ~60x faster, but MTEB retrieval 31 vs 43) as an opt-in "lite" tier; sentence-transformers costs ~217 MB (torch 124 MB) and is only justifiable as a developer extra. Expected CPU-only indexing on the 6-core 8640HS: a few hundred chunks/s with int8 MiniLM (tens of minutes for 100k emails), seconds with static embeddings; OCR and the PRD's LLM triple extraction, not embedding, will dominate ingest time.


### Recommended

Core = SQLite (stdlib) for all metadata + FTS5 keyword index, vectors in sqlite-vec (0.3 MB, zero deps) or an fp16 numpy memmap per project, embeddings from a bundled int8 ONNX all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (23 MB; or int8 bge-small-en-v1.5, 34 MB, if the extra quality is worth 2x slower indexing) run by onnxruntime + tokenizers, shipped as a separate PyPI "models" wheel so the whole install is pip-only, offline, ~55-65 MB, with no post-install downloads and no compiler. Exact search over <=300k chunks is 10-30 ms, metadata filters (dates, sender, project, OR/LIKE) are plain SQL intersected with the vector scan, and every engine stays swappable behind a tiny interface. Offer potion-base-8M (model2vec) as a `[lite]` extra and usearch/voyager as a `[bigindex]` extra; drop chromadb from the core (85 packages incl. kubernetes/grpc, S3 model download) or keep it only as an optional `[chroma]` backend fed by your own embedder.


### Alternatives

  - LanceDB 0.40 — one ~129 MB dependency (83 MB Rust wheel + pyarrow) gives vectors, full SQL WHERE incl. OR/LIKE with prefilter, FTS and scalar indexes with no glue code; heavier download/RAM than SQLite+numpy and telemetry-freeness is inferred from code absence rather than a stated policy.
  - chromadb 1.5.9 (PRD choice) — familiar API, $and/$or filters, no product telemetry since 1.5.4, Rust core with bundled SQLite; but ~85 packages/~83 MB (kubernetes, grpcio, opentelemetry, uvicorn) and the default embedding function fetches an 83 MB tarball from S3 unless you pre-seed ~/.cache/chroma or supply your own EF.
  - faiss-cpu 1.15.1 IndexFlatIP + IDSelector — 16 MB wheel, exact search, robust precomputed-id filtering; a second native dependency doing what numpy already does at this scale.
  - usearch 2.26.5 or voyager 2.2.2 HNSW — <1 MB wheels for a >500k-vector tier; usearch has no Python filter predicates so filtering is post-hoc, and both add index-file lifecycle management for the 1:1 mirror sync.
  - sentence-transformers 6.2 — best model zoo and tooling, but torch 124 MB / ~217 MB total on Windows CPU; only as a developer or 'full' extra.
  - model2vec potion-base-8M as default embedder — 30 MB, numpy-only math, ~60x faster indexing; MTEB retrieval 31 vs 43 for MiniLM makes it a lite tier, not the default.


### Install Footprint

Recommended core (cp312 win_amd64 wheel sizes from PyPI JSON): numpy 12.6 MB + onnxruntime 14.9 MB + tokenizers 2.9 MB + sqlite-vec 0.3 MB ≈ 31 MB, plus a separate models wheel with int8 all-MiniLM-L6-v2 ≈ 23 MB (or int8 bge-small ≈ 34 MB, or potion-base-8M ≈ 30 MB) → ≈ 55-65 MB total and zero downloads after pip. Optional: onnxruntime-directml +25 MB, usearch/voyager +0.3 MB, model2vec +~9 MB on top of numpy/tokenizers. PRD option: chromadb 1.5.9 ≈ 83 MB across ~85 packages (chromadb 23.5, onnxruntime 14.9, numpy 12.6, kubernetes 7.5, grpcio 5.2, ...) + 83 MB onnx.tar.gz pulled from S3 at first use (≈87 MB extracted in %USERPROFILE%\.cache\chroma) ≈ 166 MB. LanceDB path ≈ 129 MB (lancedb 83.4 + pyarrow 28.6 + numpy 12.6 + ...) plus the embedding runtime. sentence-transformers path ≈ 217 MB (torch 124.1, scipy 36.7, transformers 12.8, ...) plus a 90 MB fp32 model. Figures are download sizes resolved from PyPI metadata, not a measured pip run; installed size is typically 2-3x larger.


### Open Questions For Owner

  - Is English-only semantic search acceptable? MiniLM/bge-small are English; a multilingual model is 0.5 GB+ and cannot be shipped on PyPI without a per-file limit increase or a chunked multi-wheel scheme.
  - Quality vs speed default: int8 all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (23 MB, 256-token chunks, ~2x faster) or int8 bge-small-en-v1.5 (34 MB, 512-token chunks, ~0.06 NDCG better)?
  - Do you accept a hand-rolled SQLite+sqlite-vec/numpy store (smallest, most auditable) or would you rather take LanceDB's ~129 MB for built-in SQL filters + FTS, or keep Chroma's API as an optional backend?
  - Are separate PyPI 'models' packages (e.g. emailsupersearch-model-minilm) acceptable, and should the default `pip install emailsupersearch` include one model or none?
  - Realistic upper bound on mailbox size (emails x attachments x chunks)? Below ~500k chunks exact search suffices; above that an HNSW extra and its index lifecycle must be designed.
  - Which Python versions must be supported? onnxruntime 1.31 needs >=3.11; recommending 3.11-3.13 only.
  - How will Python itself get onto the no-admin laptop (python.org per-user installer vs Microsoft Store Python)? This affects sqlite3 extension loading, pythonw.exe availability and where user-level pip puts scripts.
  - Is an optional GPU path via onnxruntime-directml (+25 MB) on the Radeon 760M worth maintaining, or is CPU-only embedding fine given OCR/LLM dominate ingest time?


### Claims

  1. [high] chromadb 1.5.9 is the latest release (uploaded 2026-05-05, requires_python>=3.9). Its Windows artifact is a single stable-ABI wheel chromadb-1.5.9-cp39-abi3-win_amd64.whl (23.5 MB), which pip accepts for cp311, cp312 and cp313 (and 3.14). Every 1.x release since 1.0.0 (2025-04-03) shipped the same cp39-abi3 win_amd64 wheel.
     -> No compiler needed for chromadb on the no-admin laptop; pin chromadb>=1.5.4,<2 and support Python 3.11-3.13 with one wheel.
  2. [high] chromadb 1.x defaults to the Rust core (chroma_api_impl = "chromadb.api.rust.RustBindingsAPI"); Chroma claims 3-5x faster local writes/queries vs the old Python/hnswlib path. The Rust side uses sqlx with the "sqlite" feature, which maps to sqlx-sqlite/bundled (libsqlite3-sys 0.30.1 is in Cargo.lock), i.e. SQLite is compiled into the wheel. chroma-hnswlib==0.7.6 appears in requires_dist only under extra == "dev".
     -> The old 'Chroma requires Python sqlite3 > 3.35' and 'Failed building wheel for chroma-hnswlib' problems do not apply to 1.x; do not add chroma-hnswlib to your requirements.
  3. [medium] chromadb's install tree on Windows/cp312 resolves to ~85 packages and ~83 MB of wheel downloads: chromadb 23.5 MB, onnxruntime 14.9, numpy 12.6, kubernetes 7.5, grpcio 5.2, hf-xet 3.8, tokenizers 2.9, pydantic-core 2.3, plus opentelemetry-sdk/api/exporter-otlp-proto-grpc, uvicorn[standard] (watchfiles, websockets, httptools), typer, rich, bcrypt, jsonschema, httpx, pypika, mmh3, orjson, pyyaml, tenacity, build.
     -> Chroma is the heaviest non-LLM component by package count (kubernetes and grpc in a desktop app will raise eyebrows in a corporate software review); if kept, make it an optional extra, not the core store. Figures come from resolving requires_dist markers against PyPI JSON (newest versions, no extras), not from a real pip run.
  4. [high] Chroma product telemetry is gone: the mintlify docs state "As of version 1.5.4, Chroma no longer collects product telemetry"; chromadb/telemetry/product/posthog.py at tags 1.5.3, 1.5.9 and main is a stub whose capture() is `pass` and never imports posthog; `posthog` is absent from requires_dist of 1.5.3/1.5.4/1.5.9; no posthog/sentry/amplitude crate exists in the Rust Cargo.lock or workspace Cargo.toml; the old docs.trychroma.com/telemetry page now returns 404 and no telemetry doc exists in the repo tree. Residuals: Settings still has `anonymized_telemetry: bool = True` (now inert) and ProductTelemetryClient still writes a UUID to ~/.cache/chroma/telemetry_user_id (no network). OpenTelemetry crates/packages are present but only export if you configure an OTLP endpoint.
     -> Zero-telemetry requirement is satisfiable with chromadb>=1.5.4; still pass Settings(anonymized_telemetry=False) for older-version safety and document that %USERPROFILE%\.cache\chroma\telemetry_user_id is a local-only artifact (or expect it to be flagged in a privacy audit).
  5. [high] Chroma's DefaultEmbeddingFunction (ONNXMiniLM_L6_V2) downloads https://chroma-onnx-models.s3.amazonaws.com/all-MiniLM-L6-v2/onnx.tar.gz (HTTP HEAD: Content-Length 83,178,821 bytes, last modified 2023-03-30; expected SHA256 913d7300ceae3b2dbc2c50d1de4baacab4be7b9380491c27fab7418616a16ec3) to Path.home()/.cache/chroma/onnx_models/all-MiniLM-L6-v2/onnx.tar.gz and extracts it to .../onnx/. It downloads only if the archive is missing or its SHA256 mismatches, and (re)extracts whenever any of config.json, model.onnx, special_tokens_map.json, tokenizer_config.json, tokenizer.json, vocab.txt is missing. There is no environment variable to relocate the cache; truncation/padding is fixed at 256 tokens; default batch size 32; it uses onnxruntime with every available provider except CoreML.
     -> Pre-seeding an air-gapped machine means writing the exact tarball (with matching SHA256) plus the six extracted files under %USERPROFILE%\.cache\chroma\onnx_models\all-MiniLM-L6-v2\; the 256-token cap also forces small chunks. Cleaner: never use the default EF; supply embeddings= yourself or register a custom EmbeddingFunction.
  6. [high] Chroma lets you bypass its embedding download entirely: collection.add(ids=, embeddings=, documents=, metadatas=) stores caller-provided vectors without calling the EF (dimensionality must match the collection), and a custom EmbeddingFunction (implement __call__, name(), get_config(), build_from_config(), decorate with @register_embedding_function) is first-class. Metadata filters support $eq (or bare value), $gt, $gte, $lte, $in, $nin, $contains/$not_contains for arrays, and $and/$or composition over str/int/float/bool values.
     -> If Chroma is retained, make it a pure vector/metadata store fed by your own ONNX embedder; its filter language already covers the PRD's range + equality + AND/OR needs.
  7. [high] hnswlib on PyPI (latest 0.8.0, 2023-12-03) has shipped only sdists for every release since 2019, so `pip install hnswlib` compiles C++ and fails on Windows without MSVC Build Tools ('Microsoft Visual C++ 14.0 or greater is required'). chroma-hnswlib 0.7.6 (2024-07-22) has win_amd64 wheels only for cp37-cp312, none for cp313. Spotify's voyager 2.2.2 (2026-10-16) is an HNSW alternative with win_amd64 wheels (cp39, cp310, cp311, cp312-abi3; 0.2 MB, zero deps).
     -> Never list hnswlib or chroma-hnswlib as a dependency; if HNSW is needed, use usearch or voyager.
  8. [high] usearch 2.26.5 (2026-10-18) ships win_amd64 wheels for cp310-cp314 (0.3-0.4 MB) with deps numpy, tqdm, numkong (0.5 MB); it supports save/load/view (memory-mapped) and multi-index search. Its README capability table marks 'Filter predicates' as unsupported in the Python binding (supported only in C++/C/Rust/Swift).
     -> usearch is the smallest HNSW option for a >500k-vector tier, but metadata filtering must be done by over-fetching k and post-filtering in SQLite/Python, or by one index per project.
  9. [high] faiss-cpu 1.15.1 (2026-09-16) ships win_amd64 wheels for cp310-cp314 (16.3 MB; deps numpy>=1.25, packaging). Subset search is done with IDSelector objects (IDSelectorRange, IDSelectorArray, IDSelectorBatch, IDSelectorBitmap, IDSelectorNot) passed via SearchParameters.sel; IndexFlat applies them efficiently.
     -> Viable exact-search engine with pre-computed id filters (SQL -> id set -> IDSelectorBatch), but it adds a 16 MB native dependency for something numpy already does at this scale.
  10. [high] sqlite-vec 0.1.9 (2026-03-31) ships sqlite_vec-0.1.9-py3-none-win_amd64.whl (0.3 MB, zero Python deps; 'pure C, no dependencies'). It is pre-v1 and brute-force only (ANN indexes are planned, issue #25). Measured: 1M x 128-d k=20 in ~33 ms (vec0), 100k x 1536-d in ~105 ms. Loading requires sqlite3.Connection.enable_load_extension(True) then sqlite_vec.load(conn); the python.org CPython 3.14 Windows build has enable_load_extension (verified locally; bundled SQLite 3.50.4), while macOS system Python lacks it.
     -> Smallest possible store; one SQLite file per project can hold metadata, FTS5 keyword index and vectors. Verify extension loading on whatever Python the office provisions (Microsoft Store Python not tested here).
  11. [high] sqlite-vec vec0 metadata filtering (since v0.1.6): up to 16 metadata columns (TEXT/INTEGER/FLOAT/BOOLEAN) usable in a KNN WHERE with =, !=, >, >=, <, <= (booleans only =/!=), `IN (...)` on INTEGER/TEXT columns (SQLite >= 3.38), combined with AND; up to 4 partition-key columns pre-shard the scan (about 3x faster in the author's example). Not supported inside the KNN WHERE: LIKE/GLOB/REGEXP/scalar functions, NULLs, auxiliary (+) columns; OR is not documented. `rowid IN (...)` is handled natively via sqlite3_vtab_in, and vec_distance_cosine/vec_distance_L2 exist as plain scalar functions for ordinary SELECTs.
     -> Covers equality + range + AND directly; for OR/LIKE/date-string filters run an ordinary SQL query over your metadata table first and feed the result as `rowid IN (...)` (or a UNION of KNN queries), or do the mask in numpy.
  12. [high] LanceDB 0.40.0 (2026-10-07, requires_python>=3.10) ships lancedb-0.40.0-cp310-abi3-win_amd64.whl (83.4 MB) and requires pyarrow>=16 (28.6 MB) and numpy, pydantic, tqdm, lance-namespace; pylance is only an optional extra. Total ~18 packages / ~129 MB. Without a vector index it performs exhaustive kNN (100% recall) and the docs say brute force 'is not scalable for datasets larger than a few hundred thousand vectors'. .where() accepts SQL with =, <, >, IN, AND, OR, NOT, LIKE, IS NULL, IS TRUE, CAST, regexp_match and DataFusion functions; pre-filtering is the default; scalar indexes BTREE (default), BITMAP, LABEL_LIST, FM speed up filters; FTS is built in. No telemetry mention in the README and no telemetry/analytics-named files in the repo tree.
     -> The one-dependency 'batteries included' alternative: richest filter language of the options (full OR/LIKE), hybrid FTS+vector, prefilter semantics, no compiler. Cost: ~129 MB download and pyarrow in-process RAM; absence of telemetry is inferred from absence of code, not from a policy statement.
  13. [high] PyPI default limits are 100 MB per uploaded file and 10 GB per project; higher per-file limits are granted via a pypi/support issue after at least one smaller release exists.
     -> Model weights can be shipped as separate pip packages as long as each wheel stays under 100 MB (int8 MiniLM 23 MB, int8 bge-small 34 MB, potion-base-8M 30 MB all fit; fp32 MiniLM 90 MB barely fits; bge-small fp32 133 MB and potion-retrieval-32M 129 MB do not).

[high] fastembed 0.9.0 (2026-10-07) is a pure-Python wheel (0.1 MB) whose Windows dependency tree is ~31 packages / ~45 MB (onnxruntime 14.9, numpy 12.6, pillow 7.4, hf-xet 3.8, tokenizers 2.9, huggingface-hub, requests, loguru, py-rust-stemmers, mmh3). Default model BAAI/bge-small-en-v1.5 is pulled from HF repo Qdrant/bge-small-en-v1.5-onnx-Q (model_optimized.onnx, size_in_GB 0.067, dim 384, MIT); the supported-models page lists all-MiniLM-L6-v2 at 0.090 GB. Cache precedence: cache_dir argument > FASTEMBED_CACHE_PATH env var > tempfile.gettempdir()/fastembed_cache. Offline: local_files_only=True, HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1/TRUE/YES/ON, or specific_model_path=<folder> which returns immediately and skips every cache/download/verification step. The old GCS bucket no longer serves built-in models.

It still pulls huggingface-hub/pillow/requests you do not need; a 60-line onnxruntime+tokenizers embedder avoids them.

MTEB (English): potion-base-8M avg 51.08 / retrieval 31.11; potion-base-32M 52.13 / 32.67; potion-retrieval-32M retrieval 35.06; all-MiniLM-L6-v2 55.93 / 42.92; GloVe 45.82 / 21.80. The project claims up to 500x faster than the source transformer on CPU; potion-base-8M is distilled from bge-base-en-v1.5, MIT.

NanoBEIR NDCG@10: bge-small 0.6267, bge-base 0.6376, all-MiniLM-L6-v2 0.5623, static-retrieval 0.5032, BM25 0.4518. The sbert efficiency guide recommends onnx or openvino-qint8 backends on CPU but publishes only relative speedups in charts.
     -> MiniLM is ~2x faster than bge-small for ~0.06 NDCG less; static models are ~60x faster than MiniLM at a further ~0.06 loss; BM25/FTS5 is a cheap, strong hybrid companion.

Public spec pages do not list AVX-512 (Zen 4 in general supports it, but it is unconfirmed for this SKU here).
     -> Ship the quint8_avx2 ONNX variant as the safe default (or let onnxruntime choose); treat the VNNI/AVX-512 variant as an optimization to benchmark, not assume.
