  17. [high] The MCP transport spec codifies the localhost threat model: "Servers MUST validate the Origin header on all incoming connections to prevent DNS rebinding attacks", "When running locally, servers SHOULD bind only to localhost (127.0.0.1) rather than all network interfaces (0.0.0.0)", "Servers SHOULD implement proper authentication for all connections"; "Without these protections, attackers could use DNS rebinding to interact with local MCP servers from remote websites."
     -> Treat Origin/Host validation + a per-session token as mandatory middleware, not an option.
  18. [high] Jupyter Server's reference implementation: token auth is "on by default"; the token can be passed as `Authorization: token ...`, `?token=...` or the login form, after which "a cookie will be set in your browser". `allow_remote_access` (default False) returns 403 "if the 'Host' header shows that the browser thinks it's on a non-local domain" to stop DNS rebinding; 127.0.0.1/::1 and `local_hostnames` (default ['localhost']) are always local. `use_redirect_file` (default True) launches the browser via a local HTML redirect file so the token is not visible on the command line/process list, but this breaks on WSL, Chromebooks and Microsoft Store Python (sandboxed paths), so provide a fallback.
     -> Copy the pattern: secrets.token_urlsafe(32) per launch, exchanged once for an HttpOnly SameSite=Strict cookie, Host allow-list {localhost,127.0.0.1,[::1]} with any port; on Windows open the tokenized URL directly (redirect-file trick is unreliable there) and print it as a fallback.
  19. [high] MLflow 3.x retrofitted exactly this: `mlflow server` defaults to 127.0.0.1:5000, the CLI says --host "is NOT a security setting - it only controls network binding", `--allowed-hosts` defaults to localhost + private IPs and rejects bad Host headers with 403 "to prevent DNS rebinding attacks", CORS blocks state-changing methods (POST/PUT/DELETE/PATCH) from non-localhost origins (LOCALHOST_ORIGIN_PATTERNS = http://localhost|127.0.0.1|[::1] on any port), and it sets X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff and X-Frame-Options; `--disable-security-middleware` is labelled DANGEROUS. On Windows it runs under waitress (--waitress-opts) instead of gunicorn and --dev is "Unsupported on Windows".
     -> Use MLflow's checklist as the acceptance test for your middleware (Host allow-list, Origin check on unsafe methods, nosniff/X-Frame-Options/CSP headers).
  20. [high] Chrome shipped Local Network Access: an opt-in blocking flag in Chrome 138, a permission prompt "launching in Chrome 142" (late 2025) gating requests from public sites to private/loopback addresses; loopback (127.0.0.0/8, ::1) is covered as a destination, WebSockets/WebTransport/WebRTC were not yet gated at 138, and Chrome plans to extend protections later to requests "from a local server to localhost". Pages served from 127.0.0.1 talking to 127.0.0.1 are not gated. Private Network Access (CORS-preflight based) was put on hold and replaced by LNA. Firefox has no equivalent yet (per the 0.0.0.0-day write-up).
     -> Browser-side protection is improving (Edge follows Chromium) but is not universal; keep the token + Origin checks and do not rely on CORS alone because 'simple' cross-site POSTs still reach the server even when the response is hidden.
  21. [high] The '0.0.0.0-day' research showed public websites could reach local servers without reading responses ("a single request can be enough to cause damage"); Chromium blocked 0.0.0.0 by Chrome 133 and WebKit followed; "Windows is not impacted by this issue". The recommended developer mitigations were: verify the Host header, add a minimal authorization layer even on localhost (Jupyter's token cited as the model), and implement CSRF tokens even for local apps.
     -> Require the session token on every non-GET request (header or SameSite cookie + custom header), and reject requests whose Origin is present and not a loopback origin.
  22. [high] Streamlit enables server.enableXsrfProtection and server.enableCORS by default; Gradio enables strict_cors by default; marimo enables token auth by default in edit mode ("Authentication is enabled by default when running marimo edit/tutorial/new"), passes it as `?access_token=` and auto-opens the browser with the tokenized URL, warning that the startup line (and any service log) then contains the token.
     -> Avoid writing the tokenized URL to persistent logs; print it once to the console and store the token only in the browser cookie and in-memory on the server (or in a 0600 file under %LOCALAPPDATA% for reconnecting tabs).
  23. [high] CPython's webbrowser module on Windows registers `windows-default` -> WindowsDefault, whose open() calls `os.startfile(url)` (the shell's default handler), plus Edge/Chrome/Firefox if found on PATH; the documented API `webbrowser.open(url, new=0, autoraise=True)` returns True if a browser launched, and the BROWSER env var can override. No elevation is involved.
     -> `webbrowser.open_new_tab(f"http://127.0.0.1:{port}/?token=...")` is sufficient for standard users; fall back to printing the URL if it returns False.
  24. [high] Entry points: console_scripts "are wrapped in a console executable" while gui_scripts "are wrapped in a GUI executable, so they can be started without a console"; "The difference ... only affects Windows systems." pip --user installs go to the user scheme (site.USER_BASE); the user Scripts directory is often not on PATH, whereas `python -m <pkg>` always works.
     -> Ship `__main__.py` (python -m emailsupersearch), a console_script for terminals and a gui_script (no console window) for the background sync/tray; document `python -m` as the canonical launch because PATH cannot be assumed on locked-down laptops.
  25. [high] Python itself can be installed per-user without admin: the python.org installer's "Install Now" states "You will not need to be an administrator (unless a system update for the C Runtime Library is required...)" and installs into the user directory; the newer Python install manager (Store/winget) installs runtimes that "are all per-user only. The Python install manager does not support installing runtimes per-machine." pythonw.exe ships with these installs.
     -> The no-admin premise holds end to end (Python -> pip --user/venv -> your wheel), provided corporate policy does not block the Store/MSIX or AppLocker does not block user-profile executables; call this out in docs.
  26. [high] pywebview 6.2.1 (2026-04-15, pure wheel 0.5 MB, requires Python >=3.10) depends on `pythonnet; sys_platform=='win32'` (pythonnet 3.2.1 supports Python 3.11-3.15 and needs .NET Framework >4.0), plus proxy_tools and bottle. On Windows the renderer order is `edgechromium` (requires the WebView2/Edge Runtime) then `mshtml` (deprecated IE11 engine, "the only renderer that is guaranteed to be available"); selection via PYWEBVIEW_GUI or webview.start(gui=...). NiceGUI's native mode is built on the same stack and notes Windows native mode "requires the .NET Framework".
     -> Offer an `[window]` extra (pywebview+pythonnet) rather than a hard dependency; detect WebView2 via the EdgeUpdate registry keys and fall back to the system browser instead of MSHTML.

Presence is detectable via the `pv` value under HKLM\SOFTWARE\WOW6432Node\Microsoft\EdgeUpdate\Clients\{F3017226-FE2A-4295-8BDF-00C3A9A7E4C5} or the HKCU equivalent. .NET Framework 4.8.1 is installed by default on Windows 11 22H2/23H2/24H2/25H2 (4.8 on 21H2).

All pystray features are available" and "If you only target Windows, calling run() from a thread other than the main thread is safe"; project shows 27 open issues/16 open PRs and no release in 3 years. infi.systray 0.1.12.1 (2025-01-11) is a lighter Windows-only alternative.

[medium] schtasks /create: `/rl` "The default value is Limited" (least privilege, Standard User); `/sc onlogon` + `/it` (run only when that user is logged on) + `/delay`; by default the task runs "with the permissions of the current user". The same page says, in the SYSTEM-account section, "Only Administrators can schedule tasks, regardless of the value of the /ru parameter" - Microsoft's wording is ambiguous and standard-user task creation is commonly restricted by corporate GPO. Microsoft's documented no-admin fallback is the per-user Startup folder (`shell:startup`), which mentions no administrator rights.

Allow access") when a program starts listening on a network-reachable address, and "Administrator privileges are required to modify program access through the Windows Firewall"; older Microsoft guidance says the exception is created automatically only if the approving user has Administrator/Network Operator rights. No Microsoft page confirming that loopback-only listeners never trigger the prompt was found; this is widely observed but unverified here.
     -> Never bind 0.0.0.0 (NiceGUI/aiohttp/Streamlit defaults) - a standard user cannot click through the firewall prompt and the app may silently become unreachable from other hosts anyway; binding 127.0.0.1 sidesteps the issue.

Its settings default to telemetry ON: `COLLECT_ANALYTICS = get_bool_env('COLLECT_ANALYTICS', ..., True)` ("Whether to send analytics telemetry data"), `LATEST_VERSION_CHECK = True`, Sentry DSNs default None, CSP allows *.ingest.sentry.io and Google analytics domains.

### Verifier
recommendation_holds=True

**Revised recommendation:** The recommendation holds: a prebuilt SPA compiled in CI and shipped as package data, served by a Starlette-family ASGI app under uvicorn, bound to 127.0.0.1:0 with a per-launch token exchanged for an HttpOnly cookie, Host/Origin validation, docs_url=None, and `python -m emailsupersearch` opening the default browser. 36 of 38 claims survived independent verification (PyPI JSON, raw GitHub sources, official docs, and a fresh wheel audit); the measured bundle sizes (mlflow 26.6 MB, khoj 15.8 MB, marimo _static 26.5 MB, open-webui frontend 237.8 MB inside a 106 MB wheel) and the telemetry defaults for Streamlit, Gradio, Reflex, Panel, Label Studio, Khoj, Open WebUI and chromadb all check out. Four adjustments: (1) FastAPI 0.162.0 now depends on opentelemetry-api and ships a native fastapi/telemetry package (inert unless OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT is set) - either use plain Starlette 1.9.0 (requires Python >=3.11) or keep FastAPI with telemetry explicitly disabled and documented. (2) Claim 20's 'Firefox has no equivalent' is out of date: Firefox 151 (May 2026) rolled out Local Network Access prompts to all users and added a LocalNetworkAccess enterprise policy covering localhost, so design strictly same-origin (SPA and API on the same 127.0.0.1:port origin) and prefer SSE/fetch for streaming with WebSockets as an optional upgrade, because both Chrome and Firefox are moving to gate cross-origin local-network connections. (3) Make the optional desktop window an Edge/Chrome `--app=` launch with an isolated --user-data-dir before falling back to pywebview (pythonnet/.NET/WebView2), which keeps the [window] extra dependency-free for standard users. (4) Do not expect 'small' from the cited templates: marimo's wheel is 120 MB uncompressed (87 MB of it the Copilot LSP) and JupyterLab duplicates its 17 MB static tree, so budget ~3-5 MB of server wheels plus your own 2-5 MB of static assets and verify with a wheel-content check in CI. Open items: the loopback-only firewall-prompt behavior (claim 30) is still unverified by any Microsoft source and should be tested empirically on the target laptop, and Streamlit/Shiny wheels carry fonts.googleapis references that matter only if those frameworks are reconsidered.

### Missed Options

  - Shiny for Python (shiny 1.8.0, 2026-09-13, 4.1 MB pure wheel / 12.5 MB on disk, requires >=3.10): the closest Python-only alternative to NiceGUI that the researcher did not evaluate; it runs on starlette + uvicorn + websockets, ships its JS/CSS inside the wheel (htmltools), has built-in sidebar/page layouts, and my wheel grep found no segment/posthog endpoints (the 204 'telemetry' matches are a shiny telemetry module name and 22 fonts.googleapis references in bundled themes that need a local-font override). Needs a proper telemetry audit before use. https://pypi.org/pypi/shiny/json
  - Mesop (mesop 1.3.7, 2026-09-26, 7.5 MB wheel / 35 MB on disk): Google's Flask-based framework with a prebuilt Angular frontend; pip-only and Windows-friendly, but the wheel contains 26 cdn.jsdelivr.net, 8 fonts.googleapis, 7 sentry and 8 analytics string hits, so it is NOT offline-clean out of the box and would need auditing. https://pypi.org/pypi/mesop/json
  - FastHTML (python-fasthtml 0.14.14, 2026-10-20, 0.08 MB, Starlette + htmx): often suggested for 'htmx + Python' apps, but it is unsuitable as-is for an offline product because fasthtml/core.py hard-codes htmx, surreal, css-scope-inline and all htmx extensions from cdn.jsdelivr.net/unpkg.com (lines 556-578); only usable if every default header script is replaced with locally served copies. This should be listed as a rejected option so nobody reaches for it. https://pypi.org/pypi/python-fasthtml/json
  - Browser 'app mode' window instead of pywebview: launch the preinstalled Edge (or Chrome) with `msedge.exe --app=http://127.0.0.1:<port> --user-data-dir=%LOCALAPPDATA%\emailsupersearch\edge-profile` via subprocess. It gives a chromeless desktop-style window with zero extra Python dependencies (no pythonnet, .NET, or WebView2 SDK detection), works for a standard user, and falls back to webbrowser.open() if the executable is not found. This is a lighter default for the proposed [window] extra than pywebview, which can remain as a secondary option. (Chromium `--app=<url>` switch; Edge is Chromium-based.) https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/answers/questions/2381569/how-install-this-site-as-an-app-using-command-line
  - Plain Starlette instead of FastAPI for the server: FastAPI 0.162.0 now adds opentelemetry-api as a hard dependency and ships a first-party fastapi/telemetry package (inert unless OTEL_* env vars are set). For a 'zero telemetry code' marketing claim and a smaller dependency tree, Starlette 1.9.0 alone (0.08 MB, requires Python >=3.11) plus pydantic for validation is the cleaner base; if FastAPI is kept, pass telemetry={'tracing': False, 'metrics': False, 'logging': False} and document it. https://pypi.org/pypi/fastapi/json
  - HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run as a third autostart path for the background sync (standard-user writable, no Task Scheduler GPO dependence, no shortcut file), alongside shell:startup and schtasks /rl LIMITED. Not verified against a Microsoft page in this session; treat as a candidate to confirm.
  - Textual + textual-serve (1.1.3, aiohttp) and Solara/Voila (ipywidgets stack) exist as pip-only browser UIs but do not fit a chat + left-pane search product and add heavy or TUI-shaped dependencies; worth naming as considered-and-rejected rather than missed.



---

## Area: windows-no-admin


### Summary

A pure "pip install" delivery is feasible for a standard user, but only if the library (a) assumes a per-user CPython (python.org "Install Now" into %LocalAppData%\Programs\Python, or the 3.14+ Python install manager, both admin-free) and never relies on console_scripts being on PATH, (b) installs into a short venv under %LOCALAPPDATA% rather than pip --user (which lands in the roaming %APPDATA% and, under Store Python, in a 142-char path that pushes torch's 121-char internal paths past MAX_PATH), and (c) treats native DLL loading as the main failure surface. Inspection of the actual Windows wheels shows torch, onnxruntime, chromadb's 63 MB Rust binding, PyMuPDF, gpt4all and llama-cpp-python all import MSVCP140.dll without bundling it, and vc_redist.x64.exe needs a UAC prompt, so a missing VC++ runtime is a hard ImportError for a non-admin; the library must preflight this and may ship app-local copies via os.add_dll_directory. AppLocker's default rules only allow %windir% and %programfiles% executables (a per-user python.exe is blocked, Store Python is not), WDAC/App Control and the Defender ASR "prevalence" rule can block unsigned .pyd/.dll/.exe from the profile, and Controlled Folder Access protects Documents, so data must live in %LOCALAPPDATA% and the install must self-diagnose. For background sync, Task Scheduler as a standard user is limited to interactive-token "run only when logged on" tasks (S4U/password need the "Log on as a batch job" right that Users lack) and schtasks /sc ONLOGON is reported to fail non-elevated, so the robust choice is an in-process watchfiles/watchdog watcher plus periodic reconcile scans, optionally auto-started via a per-user Startup-folder pythonw shortcut. Several PRD components violate the offline/no-telemetry constraint by default (chromadb telemetry on, chromadb/gpt4all/EasyOCR auto-download models into ~/.cache) and llama-cpp-python has no Windows wheels on PyPI at all, which forces a decision about vendoring the LLM engine.


### Recommended

Ship one PyPI package with extras ([ocr], [llm], [graph]) and a bootstrap recipe of: per-user CPython from python.org or the 3.14 install manager -> 'py -m venv %LOCALAPPDATA%\eMailSuperSearch\venv' (short, non-roaming, not CFA-protected) -> 'venv\Scripts\python -m pip install emailsupersearch[...]' -> launch with 'python -m emailsupersearch' (no console_script dependency). Root all state in %LOCALAPPDATA%\eMailSuperSearch, force ANONYMIZED_TELEMETRY=False/HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1 and explicit model paths, and run the .msg watcher (watchfiles) plus a periodic reconcile scan inside the web-server process, with an optional per-user Startup-folder pythonw shortcut instead of any Task Scheduler task. Add an 'emailsupersearch doctor' preflight that checks msvcp140/msvcp140_1/vcomp140/vulkan-1 presence, site-packages path headroom, and imports each native module in a subprocess to produce an IT-ready report when AppLocker/WDAC/ASR blocks DLLs. Replace gpt4all with llama-cpp-python's Vulkan wheel (extra-index-url or a self-published vendored wheel) and easyocr with an onnxruntime-based OCR to cut ~700 MB and the torch MAX_PATH hazard.


### Alternatives

  - Microsoft Store Python + pip --user — admin-free and its python.exe is allowed under AppLocker default rules, but AppData writes are redirected into LocalCache, py.exe is absent, Scripts is off PATH, the 142-char site-packages prefix breaks torch under MAX_PATH, and the Store is often disabled by policy.
  - Python install manager (3.14 MSIX) + offline index — cleanest per-user model with 'py install --download/--source' for air-gapped installs, but MSIX/Store/winget may be blocked and it is new (2025), so the legacy .exe installer must remain a documented fallback.
  - uv-managed Python + 'uv tool install emailsupersearch' — zero admin and offline-capable with a lockfile, but requires running a PowerShell installer (-ExecutionPolicy ByPass) or GitHub download, defaults Pythons/tools into roaming %APPDATA%, and adds a second tool for IT to approve.
  - Per-user Task Scheduler task (COM/XML, InteractiveToken, LeastPrivilege) — keeps sync alive after the UI exits, but a standard user cannot get 'run whether logged on or not' (needs Log on as batch job), schtasks /sc ONLOGON is reported to fail non-elevated, and tasks are visible/controllable by IT policy.
  - Startup-folder pythonw shortcut — trivially per-user and reversible, but only runs after logon and the user can disable it in Task Manager; must point at the real pythonw.exe (alias path under Store Python).
  - Embeddable Python + pre-built site-packages zip ('portable' bundle) — immune to PATH/launcher/Store quirks and lets you ship msvcp140 app-local, but violates the owner's 'only pip' constraint and still cannot bypass AppLocker/WDAC.


### Install Footprint

PRD stack (cp312 win_amd64 wheels, download / unpacked): torch 124 MB / 476 MB + torchvision 1.4 MB + easyocr 2.9 MB + opencv-python-headless 44 MB / 118 MB + scipy 37 MB / 108 MB + scikit-image 12 MB / 22 MB + numpy 12.6 MB / 42 MB + pandas 10.7 MB / 39 MB + PyMuPDF 63 MB / 104 MB + chromadb 23.5 MB / 67 MB + onnxruntime 14.9 MB / 44 MB + tokenizers 2.9 MB / 8 MB + grpcio 5.2 MB + gpt4all 119.6 MB / 221 MB (of which ~210 MB are unused CUDA DLLs on an AMD iGPU) + pywin32 6.9 MB / 15 MB + pure deps (extract-msg, python-docx, openpyxl, networkx, pyvis, kubernetes, opentelemetry) ~10 MB => roughly 490 MB to download, ~1.3 GB on disk, plus Llama 3 8B Instruct Q4_0 GGUF ~4.7 GB, EasyOCR models ~100 MB and chroma's MiniLM ONNX ~80 MB => ~6.2 GB in the user profile. Recommended stack: onnxruntime 14.9 MB / 44 MB + numpy 12.6 MB / 42 MB + PyMuPDF 63 MB / 104 MB + pandas 10.7 MB / 39 MB + chromadb 23.5 MB / 67 MB (+tokenizers/grpcio ~8 MB) or sqlite-vec 0.3 MB instead + rapidocr ~15 MB (pure, models included) + llama-cpp-python Vulkan 42.1 MB / 204 MB + watchfiles 0.3 MB + pure deps ~10 MB => roughly 190 MB to download, ~0.5 GB on disk before models; plus an ONNX embedding model (~90 MB for MiniLM-class) and the LLM GGUF (~4.7 GB for Llama 3 8B Q4_0; ~2 GB if a 3B model is chosen) => ~5.3 GB total, or ~2.6 GB with a 3B model. Offline 'pip download' bundle for the recommended stack is therefore ~190 MB of wheels plus models.


### Open Questions For Owner

  - Which Python will the target laptops actually have or be allowed to get: python.org per-user .exe, the 3.14 install manager MSIX, Microsoft Store Python, or none (and is the Store/winget blocked by policy)? A 5-minute preflight on 2-3 real corporate laptops would settle this and the next three questions.
  - Are AppLocker, App Control for Business (WDAC) or Defender ASR rules enforced on these laptops, and is Controlled Folder Access on? If unsigned .pyd/.dll from the user profile are blocked, no pip-only design can work without an IT allow rule for the install path - is requesting such a rule acceptable?
  - Is the Visual C++ 2015-2022 runtime (msvcp140.dll, msvcp140_1.dll, vcomp140.dll) present on the standard corporate image? If not, may we ship app-local copies in a helper wheel (Microsoft limits redistribution to licensed Visual Studio users)?
  - Must the Vulkan LLM engine be installable from PyPI alone (then we publish our own vendored llama.cpp wheel, ~42 MB), or is 'pip install ... --extra-index-url https://abetlen.github.io/llama-cpp-python/whl/vulkan' acceptable? Alternatively, is CPU-only inference acceptable as the default?
  - Does background sync need to run when the web UI/server is not running (requires a Startup-folder shortcut or scheduled task), or is 'sync while the app runs + reconcile at every launch' sufficient?
  - Where does the .msg archive live: local NTFS, a UNC/mapped network share, or an OneDrive-synced folder (Files On-Demand placeholders)? This decides polling vs ReadDirectoryChangesW and whether hashing files may trigger cloud downloads.
  - Are roaming profiles or disk quotas in use, and is ~5-6 GB in %LOCALAPPDATA% (venv + models + index) acceptable? Should the LLM weights be a smaller 3B model to stay under 3 GB?
  - How will model weights (embedding ONNX, LLM GGUF, OCR models) reach an air-gapped laptop with zero external calls - via an IT-approved share, USB, or an optional one-time download step on a connected machine?
  - Is requiring Python 3.12 or 3.13 acceptable (numpy 2.5/networkx 3.7 need 3.12+, onnxruntime 1.31 needs 3.11+)?
  - Is it acceptable to drop EasyOCR (torch-based, ~500 MB, MAX_PATH risk) for an onnxruntime-based OCR such as rapidocr, and to drop gpt4all for llama-cpp-python?


### Claims

  1. [high] The python.org 'Install Now' (legacy) installer is per-user by default (InstallAllUsers=0), installs to %LocalAppData%\Programs\Python\PythonXY and 'You will not need to be an administrator (unless a system update for the C Runtime Library is required...)'. PrependPath/AppendPath default to 0, so python/Scripts are not on PATH by default. The legacy installer is deprecated in 3.14 and will stop being released with 3.16 (PEP 773 accepted 25-Apr-2025).
     -> Document the python.org per-user install as the primary zero-admin bootstrap; never assume python.exe or Scripts are on PATH; prefer the py launcher ('py -m emailsupersearch').
  2. [high] Python 3.14+ ships the 'Python install manager' (pymanager) as an MSIX via python.org/Store/winget; 'an MSIX does not require administrative privileges to install'; runtimes are 'per-user only' ('does not support installing runtimes per-machine'); global commands live in %LocalAppData%\Python\bin; it supports offline installs ('py install --download=<PATH>' then 'py install --source="<PATH>\index.json"'); it provides 'py', 'python', 'pythonw', 'pyw' and 'pymanager' commands. Admins can lock settings via registry/group policy. A legacy py.exe launcher takes priority over pymanager's 'py'.
     -> Support 'py -m emailsupersearch' under both launchers; offer an offline bootstrap recipe using 'py install --download'; detect pymanager vs legacy launcher in the doctor command.
  3. [high] Microsoft Store Python redirects writes to well-known folders/registry into a private copy: writing to C:\Users\<user>\AppData\Local actually writes to ...\AppData\Local\Packages\PythonSoftwareFoundation.Python.3.x_qbz5n2kfra8p0\LocalCache\Local\; reads fall back to the real folder; 'If your scripts must modify the shared locations, you will need to install the full installer'; CPython maintainers say newer Windows builds redirect only newly created files and 'All of this is Windows, not us'. Store pip --user installs go to ...\LocalCache\local-packages\Python3XX\site-packages with a Scripts dir that pip warns is 'not on PATH'. The Store package does not include py.exe ('The py.exe launcher will detect this Python installation, but will prefer installations from the traditional installer').
     -> Detect Store Python (sys.prefix under WindowsApps/PythonSoftwareFoundation) and show the os.path.realpath() of the data dir; file:/// hyperlinks and attachment extraction paths must use the real path; prefer python.org/pymanager in docs; 'py -m' launch is unavailable under Store Python so document 'python -m' too.

[medium] Windows 11 ships 'python.exe'/'python3.exe' App Execution Alias stubs that print 'Python was not found; run without arguments to install from the Microsoft Store, or disable this shortcut from Settings > Apps > Advanced app settings > App execution aliases' when no Python is installed; enterprises can disable the Store app via the 'Turn off the Store application' policy (Enterprise/Education editions), and a pymanager issue documents a winget install that stalled at 98% on a device with the Store disabled by IT policy.

ASR rules are available on any Windows edition with Defender AV; the rule's trusted list is Microsoft-managed.

Administrators and Backup Operators group users have this privilege enabled by default' (standard users get 0x80070569 'has not been granted the requested logon type'). 'From a low privilege process, you cannot register a task with the RunLevel property equal to TASK_RUNLEVEL_HIGHEST... You are not allowed to register the task as Builtin/Administrator, Local System, or for a group.' Members of the Users group 'can only read, update, delete, and run the tasks that they have created'.
     -> A standard user can only get an interactive-token task that runs while they are logged on, at least-privilege run level, for their own account; 'run whether user is logged on or not' is effectively unavailable, so a scheduled task buys nothing over an in-process watcher except survival across UI restarts.
  21. [medium] schtasks.exe: '/sc ONLOGON - Specifies that the task runs whenever a user (any user) logs on'; '/it' sets interactive-only; '/rl' defaults to LIMITED; '/rp' - 'If you don't use this parameter when specifying a user account, SchTasks.exe will prompt for the password'; '/xml' imports a task definition. A reproducible community report shows 'schtasks /create ... /sc ONLOGON /RU Domain\User' returning 'ERROR: Access is denied' for a standard user while the same command works elevated and time-based triggers work; workarounds are '/create /xml', the Schedule.Service COM object, or PowerShell Register-ScheduledTask.

[high] watchfiles 1.3.0 (2026-09-21) ships cp310-abi3 win_amd64 wheels (0.3 MB) built on the Rust notify crate; polling is forced only when WATCHFILES_FORCE_POLLING is set or WSL is detected ('otherwise, we enable force polling only if we detect we're running on WSL'); defaults debounce=1600 ms, step=50 ms, rust_timeout=1000 ms on Windows. watchdog 6.0.0 (2024-11) uses ReadDirectoryChangesW on Windows with caveats: renames are converted to moves, directory moves may be reported before I/O completes so contents may be missed, directory deletes may appear as file deletes; a polling observer exists as fallback. ReadDirectoryChangesW itself: if the buffer overflows 'the entire contents of the buffer are discarded and the lpBytesReturned parameter will be zero' / ERROR_NOTIFY_ENUM_DIR ('you should compute the changes by enumerating the directory or subtree'), it 'fails with ERROR_INVALID_PARAMETER when the buffer length is greater than 64 KB and the application is monitoring a directory over the network', and 'If the network redirector or the target file system does not support this operation, the function fails with ERROR_INVALID_FUNCTION'.
     -> A 1:1 mirror cannot rely on events alone: run watchfiles inside the server process for low-latency updates AND a periodic/at-startup reconcile scan (compare path+size+mtime+hash of .msg files vs index); auto-enable polling when the folder is a UNC/mapped network path; this removes the need for Task Scheduler entirely.

Roaming User Profiles exclude 'Appdata\Local and Appdata\LocalLow folders and all their subfolders' by default (ExcludeProfileDirs); everything else in the profile - %APPDATA%, Documents, and dotfiles like %USERPROFILE%\.cache - roams and inflates logon time. platformdirs on Windows maps user_data_dir to %USERPROFILE%\AppData\Local\$appauthor\$appname (roaming=False), user_cache_dir to ...\Cache, user_log_dir to ...\Logs, user_runtime_dir to %LOCALAPPDATA%\Temp\...
     -> Root everything (SQLite/Chroma DB, logs, model weights, venv) at %LOCALAPPDATA%\eMailSuperSearch (platformdirs.user_data_dir('eMailSuperSearch', roaming=False)); keep only a tiny config/project list in %APPDATA% if roaming settings are desired.

     -> Never use chromadb's default embedding function (supply your own ONNX embedder with explicit, bundled model path); set ANONYMIZED_TELEMETRY=False, HF_HUB_OFFLINE=1, HF_HOME, EASYOCR_MODULE_PATH and gpt4all/llama model_path to %LOCALAPPDATA%\eMailSuperSearch\models at process start; ship models as separate optional wheels or a documented offline fetch step.

[high] OneDrive Known Folder Move redirects Desktop, Documents, Pictures (plus Screenshots/Camera Roll) and admins can 'Silently move Windows known folders to OneDrive' with no user interaction (Microsoft recommends it for enterprises); Controlled Folder Access protects by default Documents, Favorites, Music, Pictures, Videos (and Public equivalents), follows KFM redirection, is off by default and blocks untrusted apps from modifying files there; %LOCALAPPDATA% is not in the default protected list. SQLite warns that background backup/sync tools can corrupt databases ('Backup or restore while a transaction is active', 'Deleting a hot journal... the database may go corrupt'), and Zotero/Tropy maintainers report corruption of SQLite files kept in OneDrive-synced folders.
     -> Never store the index/DB under Documents/Desktop; if the user chooses a custom data path, refuse paths containing '\OneDrive' or under known folders and explain why; Excel exports with file:/// links may go to Documents since they are write-once.
  27. [high] Current PyPI facts (fetched 2026-10-23): extract-msg 0.56.1 (pure); PyMuPDF 2.0 (2026-10-19) requires Python >=3.11, cp311-abi3 win_amd64 63.0 MB (104 MB unpacked, bundles ONNX layout models) and now depends on onnxruntime, networkx, numpy; python-docx 1.2.0 (pure); easyocr 1.7.2 (2024-09-24, pure) depends on torch, torchvision, opencv-python-headless, scipy, scikit-image, shapely, pyclipper; chromadb 1.5.9 (2026-05-05) cp39-abi3 23.5 MB wheel (67 MB unpacked, one 63 MB chromadb_rust_bindings.pyd) requiring onnxruntime, tokenizers, grpcio, opentelemetry-*, kubernetes; networkx 3.7 requires >=3.12; pyvis 0.3.2 (2023); pandas 3.1.0 requires >=3.11 (cp311-cp315); openpyxl 3.1.5; onnxruntime 1.31.0 (2026-10-09) requires >=3.11, cp311-cp314 win_amd64 14.9 MB; onnxruntime-directml 1.24.4 cp311-cp314 (DirectML 'in sustained engineering'); torch 2.14.1 cp310-cp314 124 MB (476 MB unpacked, CPU-only on PyPI); numpy 2.5.4 requires >=3.12; watchdog 6.0.0 py3-none-win_amd64; pywin32 312 cp39-cp315; rapidocr-onnxruntime 1.4.4 requires <3.13 but its successor rapidocr 3.10.0 (2026-10-08) is a pure wheel for >=3.8; sqlite-vec 0.1.9 py3-none-win_amd64 0.3 MB.
     -> Target CPython 3.12-3.13 x64 (requires-python >=3.11,<3.14 to be safe); onnxruntime is already an unavoidable dependency via PyMuPDF 2.0 and chromadb, so an onnxruntime-based OCR (rapidocr) is nearly free while easyocr adds ~500 MB of torch-stack and the MAX_PATH risk.
  28. [medium] The Vulkan loader vulkan-1.dll that gpt4all's Kompute DLL and llama-cpp-python's ggml-vulkan.dll import is 'delivered with the vendor's driver package' (installed by the GPU driver, not by pip).
     -> Preflight ctypes.WinDLL('vulkan-1.dll'); if absent (stripped corporate driver image), fall back to the CPU ggml backend automatically and tell the user why GPU acceleration is off.


### Verifier
recommendation_holds=True

**Revised recommendation:** The recommendation holds in substance: one PyPI package with [ocr]/[llm]/[graph] extras, per-user python.org CPython or the 3.14 install manager, venv at %LOCALAPPDATA%\eMailSuperSearch\venv, 'python -m emailsupersearch' launch, all state under %LOCALAPPDATA%, in-process watchfiles + periodic reconcile, Startup-folder pythonw autostart, a 'doctor' preflight, llama-cpp-python Vulkan via a self-published vendored wheel instead of gpt4all, and onnxruntime-based OCR instead of easyocr. Apply these corrections: (1) the bootstrap instructions must tell standard users to untick 'Use admin privileges when installing py.exe' (InstallLauncherAllUsers=1 and Include_launcher=1 are defaults) or pass InstallLauncherAllUsers=0 Include_launcher=0, otherwise the 'per-user' install still raises a UAC prompt; keep the legacy installer documented because MSIX sideloading can be policy-blocked. (2) Add `--only-binary=:all:` to the install line and ship/point to a Vulkan wheel so pip never tries to compile the sdist. (3) Extend the doctor's DLL list to msvcp140.dll, msvcp140_1.dll, msvcp140_atomic_wait.dll, vcruntime140_1.dll, vcruntime140_threads.dll, vcomp140.dll and vulkan-1.dll, and offer `pip install msvc-runtime` into the base interpreter as the no-admin remediation instead of only producing an IT report. (4) Drop ANONYMIZED_TELEMETRY as a requirement for chromadb >= 1.5.4 (product telemetry is a no-op); the real network hazard is chromadb's default embedding function downloading from S3 to ~/.cache/chroma, so always pass an explicit local embedding function and model path. (5) Use awatch() or set rust_timeout explicitly; sync watch() defaults to 5000 ms on Windows. (6) Optionally add HKCU\...\Run as the autostart mechanism and evaluate onnxruntime-genai-directml as an all-PyPI, no-Vulkan GPU alternative for the Radeon 760M. (7) Treat gpt4all as retired: 2.8.2 is unmaintained since 2024-08, carries 210 MB of CUDA DLLs irrelevant to AMD, and loads vulkan-1.dll dynamically so failures appear late.
