### Missed Options

  - `pip install msvc-runtime` (cgohlke, PyPI 14.51.36231 uploaded 2026-10-22, cp312–cp315 win_amd64 wheels ~2 MB): a pure-pip, no-admin fix for the MSVCP140 problem. The wheel drops msvcp140.dll, msvcp140_1.dll, msvcp140_2.dll, msvcp140_atomic_wait.dll, msvcp140_codecvt_ids.dll, vcomp140.dll, concrt140.dll, vccorlib140.dll, vcamp140.dll, vcruntime140_threads.dll (plus vcruntime140/140_1 in Scripts) into sys.prefix and Scripts. Installed into the per-user BASE interpreter (not only the venv, because a venv's python.exe re-executes the base python.exe, whose directory is the 'application directory' Windows searches), it satisfies torch/onnxruntime/chromadb/PyMuPDF/llama.cpp imports with no vc_redist.exe. License is Microsoft's proprietary redist terms; the 'doctor' should suggest it as remediation, and the doctor's DLL list should include msvcp140_1.dll, msvcp140_atomic_wait.dll and vcruntime140_threads.dll (torch 2.14.1/onnxruntime 1.31 need them), not just msvcp140/vcomp140. Sources: https://pypi.org/pypi/msvc-runtime/json (wheel contents inspected), https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/cpp/windows/redistributing-visual-cpp-files
  - `pip install --only-binary=:all:` (or PIP_ONLY_BINARY=:all:) in the documented install line so that a missing/blocked extra index for llama-cpp-python fails fast instead of pip attempting to build the 76.6 MB sdist (which needs Visual Studio/CMake the office laptop will not have). Source: https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/cli/pip_download/ / https://pypi.org/pypi/llama-cpp-python/json
  - DirectML path entirely from PyPI for the Radeon 760M: onnxruntime-genai-directml 0.14.1 (cp311–cp314 win_amd64, 3.9 MB, bundles D3D12Core.dll) + onnxruntime-directml 1.24.4 (bundles DirectML.dll) run ONNX-exported Llama-3-8B-Instruct int4 on the iGPU with no Vulkan loader, no extra index and no direct-URL dependency. Trade-offs: DirectML is 'in sustained engineering' (ORT docs), genai-directml lags the CPU package (0.14.1 vs onnxruntime-genai 0.18.0, 2026-10-14), model format is ONNX not GGUF. Worth a benchmark against the vendored llama-cpp Vulkan wheel. Sources: https://pypi.org/pypi/onnxruntime-genai-directml/json, https://pypi.org/pypi/onnxruntime-directml/json, https://onnxruntime.ai/docs/execution-providers/DirectML-ExecutionProvider.html
  - HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run registry value as the per-user autostart instead of (or in addition to) a Startup-folder .lnk: same semantics (runs at that user's logon, no admin), no shell link to craft, removable with one `reg delete`; Microsoft notes both Run and Startup group may be delayed by the system. Source: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/setupapi/run-and-runonce-registry-keys
  - `pip install uv` from PyPI (uv 0.13.5, py3-none-win_amd64 15.4 MB) if uv's lockfile/offline features are wanted, avoiding the `powershell -ExecutionPolicy ByPass` installer and giving IT a single artefact source (PyPI); set UV_PYTHON_INSTALL_DIR/UV_TOOL_DIR under %LOCALAPPDATA% to avoid roaming. Source: https://pypi.org/pypi/uv/json, https://docs.astral.sh/uv/reference/storage/
  - Miniforge 'Just Me' install + conda-forge `vc14_runtime` package (MSVC runtimes installed inside the env without admin) as an IT-friendly fallback when python.org installers are blocked but conda-forge is mirrored internally; violates the 'only pip' letter but solves the msvcp140 problem natively. Source: https://anaconda.org/conda-forge/vc14_runtime
  - pymanager `py install --target=<dir>` (PEP 773: extracts a single runtime without registering it) as a middle ground between the embeddable zip and a registered install when App Execution Alias / legacy py.exe conflicts (pymanager#315) bite. Sources: https://peps.python.org/pep-0773/, https://github.com/python/pymanager/issues/315
  - Risk the researcher did not list for the pymanager/MSIX route: organisations can block sideloading of signed MSIX via the 'Allow all trusted apps to install' policy, and then 'users can't even manually enable sideloading'; the legacy .exe installer with InstallLauncherAllUsers=0 Include_launcher=0 must remain the documented admin-free fallback until 3.16. Source: https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/application-management/sideload-apps-in-windows
  - Licensing flag relevant to a PyPI library: PyMuPDF 2.0 is 'Dual Licensed - GNU AFFERO GPL 3.0 or Artifex Commercial License' and now hard-depends on onnxruntime/networkx/numpy and bundles ~51 MB of layout ONNX models; making [pdf] an optional extra (or using pypdf/pdfminer for text-only PDFs) keeps the core wheel small and AGPL-free. Source: PyMuPDF 2.0 wheel METADATA via https://pypi.org/pypi/PyMuPDF/json



---

## Completeness critique (cross-area)


### Gaps

  * The owner's 'projects' concept (constraint 3) has no data model anywhere in the seven areas. Nobody says whether a project is (a) a saved search/filter over one shared index, (b) its own set of watched .msg/.eml folders with its own index and graph, or (c) a workspace that also holds chat history, exports and graph views. The PRD itself (4.1) assumes a single 'target directory'.
    why: It determines the storage layout (one SQLite file per project vs. one global DB with project_id), whether the 1:1 mirror runs once or per project, whether the LLM/graph enrichment is per project (knowledge-graph recommends 'per-project or on-demand'), what the left search pane lists, and how exports/open-in-Outlook resolve paths. Every other decision in the digest is downstream of this.
    fix: Define: project = {name, one or more source folders (local or UNC), language, embedder id, retention of chats/exports}; one SQLite file per project under %LOCALAPPDATA%\eMailSuperSearch\projects\<id>\ (manifest, FTS5, vectors, graph tables, chat log); a global catalog DB listing projects. Allow two projects to watch the same folder (dedupe by file hash at the parser cache level). Specify the left pane as: project switcher on top, saved searches + filters below, results and chat in the main pane, graph as a tab.
  * Supported Python version range is never decided and the per-area floors contradict: networkx 3.7 needs >=3.12 (knowledge-graph pins <3.7 for 3.11), Starlette 1.9 / onnxruntime 1.31 / onnxruntime-directml 1.24.4 need >=3.11, msvc-runtime ships only cp312-cp315 (verified: https://pypi.org/pypi/msvc-runtime/json), pywin32 312 and real-ladybug stop at cp313, winrt-Windows.Media.Ocr stops at cp314 (https://pypi.org/pypi/winrt-Windows.Media.Ocr/json), spaCy 3.8.16 has cp310-cp314 win_amd64 wheels and requires <3.15 (https://pypi.org/pypi/spacy/json).
    why: On a corporate laptop the user installs whatever python.org build IT allows; a wheel-less version makes pip try to compile (no MSVC) and the whole 'only pip' story collapses. The msvc-runtime remediation that windows-no-admin recommends does not exist for 3.11 at all.
    fix: Declare Requires-Python >=3.12,<3.14 for v1 (3.12 and 3.13 are the only versions where every proposed wheel exists, including msvc-runtime), ship `--only-binary=:all:` in the documented install line, and add a CI matrix job that resolves every extra on win_amd64 for each supported version and fails if any sdist is pulled.
  * Whether a 4.7 GB GGUF can actually be allocated through Vulkan on this APU is unresolved. On AMD APUs under Windows the BIOS 'UMA Frame Buffer' (often 512 MB-4 GB, locked by IT) is the DEVICE_LOCAL heap; the rest is WDDM shared memory. llama.cpp added GGML_VK_PREFER_HOST_MEMORY (PR #11592, Feb 2025) and changed its UMA default in PR #22930 (merged 2026-06-16, later reverted by some forks: https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/pull/22930); Strix Halo / Lunar Lake users still report allocation failures with free memory (https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/18946, https://github.com/ggml-org/llama.cpp/issues/15120). The llm-engine area quotes throughput but never checks allocation behavior or whether abetlen 0.3.36 contains #22930.
    why: If the Vulkan path fails to allocate on a 512 MB frame buffer, the user sees 'out of device memory' and nothing works unless the library falls back; and host-visible memory on an iGPU can be slower than the Linux benchmark numbers quoted everywhere (which are RADV, not the Windows AMD driver).
    fix: Make the engine layer self-tuning: probe vkGetPhysicalDeviceMemoryProperties at first launch (device-local vs host-visible heap sizes), try GPU load, on failure retry with GGML_VK_PREFER_HOST_MEMORY=1, then with n_gpu_layers partial offload, then CPU. Record the chosen mode in the doctor report. Benchmark on the real 8640HS with Windows AMD driver before publishing any t/s claims; pin the llama.cpp commit in the vendored wheel and note whether it includes #22930.
  * No area sets a GPU driver / Vulkan loader floor or handles the 'no usable Vulkan' case. vulkan-1.dll and the AMD driver are IT-managed; a standard user cannot update them, and some corporate images run OEM drivers a year old or the Microsoft Basic Display Adapter. llama.cpp Vulkan requires a Vulkan 1.2+ driver with specific extensions; gpt4all's doctor-free late failure was noted but no minimum was stated for the replacement.
    why: The GPU opt-in extra will be the most frequent support ticket; the user has no way to fix a driver.
    fix: Doctor check: enumerate Vulkan devices via the loader, read apiVersion and driverVersion, require >=1.2 and the extensions ggml-vulkan asks for, and otherwise silently select the CPU wheel. Ship both CPU and Vulkan builds and choose at runtime, rather than making the user pick an extra.
  * The PRD's pythonw.exe background process (4.7) collides with how the chosen native libraries log. Under pythonw, sys.stdout/sys.stderr are None (print raises), and llama.cpp/ggml, onnxruntime and rapidocr write to C-level stderr (fd 2), which is invalid in a windowless process; uvicorn also configures console handlers by default. No area mentions this.
    why: Silent crashes or hangs of the invisible sync process, exactly the kind of failure 4.7's rotating logs are meant to capture.
    fix: At process start: open the rotating log file, dup2 its fd onto 1 and 2, replace sys.stdout/stderr with file objects, set llama_log_set to a Python callback, set ORT severity via SessionOptions.log_severity_level and onnxruntime.set_default_logger_severity, and disable uvicorn's default console log config. Prefer running headless as `python.exe` with CREATE_NO_WINDOW from the launcher instead of pythonw.exe.
  * 4.7 Rotational Logging is not covered by any area, and the privacy dimension is missing: a 5 MB x 4 log that captures 'OCR crash traces' will contain email subjects, file names, recipient addresses and possibly body snippets, stored in the profile and likely attached to IT tickets via the proposed 'doctor' report.
    why: The product's whole premise is confidentiality; logs are the easiest leak and the doctor report is designed to be handed to IT.
    fix: Define log levels and a redaction policy (hash or truncate paths/subjects at INFO, full detail only at DEBUG behind an explicit toggle), one RotatingFileHandler per process (server, sync worker, OCR worker, LLM worker) under %LOCALAPPDATA%\eMailSuperSearch\logs, and make the doctor report contain only environment facts, never per-message data.
  * Process topology is undefined and the areas pull in different directions: windows-no-admin puts the watcher inside the web-server process; ocr-attachments says OCR backends must live in a separate worker process (a combined winrt + rapidocr process segfaulted twice); knowledge-graph wants LLM enrichment opt-in and resumable; PRD 5.1 wants graphing asynchronous; the PRD vision wants sync to run 'invisibly' even when no UI is open; and the LLM used for chat and for enrichment is the same 6 GB resident object competing for the same iGPU.
    why: Without a defined supervisor, job queue and SQLite concurrency model (WAL, single writer, busy_timeout), the first release will deadlock or corrupt the manifest when a reconcile scan, an OCR worker and a chat request touch the same DB, and the chat will stall while ingestion holds the GPU.
    fix: One long-lived 'host' process (the ASGI server, auto-started at logon, tray-optional) owning the SQLite writer and a persistent job queue table; short-lived worker subprocesses for parse+OCR (isolated, restartable, per-file timeout) and one LLM worker that is either serving chat or running enrichment, never both; enrichment pauses when a chat request arrives. Document that sync runs only while the host process runs, and show its state in the UI.
  * Corporate network reality for the 'initial pip download' is unaddressed: most enterprises route pip through an Artifactory/Nexus PyPI mirror and a TLS-inspecting proxy, and block github.io, huggingface.co and raw.githubusercontent.com. Three recommended artifacts are off-PyPI: the abetlen Vulkan wheel (GitHub Pages index), GGUF weights (Hugging Face), and the proposed official llama.cpp zip fallback (GitHub releases).
    why: Constraint 1 says the only thing the user installs is the library via pip; if a required artifact is not on the mirrored index the install simply fails, and the user cannot change proxy or CA settings. The official meta-llama repos are additionally gated (verified 'gated: manual' on meta-llama/Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct), so an HF account/token would be needed unless a third-party GGUF repo is used (bartowski/QuantFactory/unsloth repos verified ungated).
    fix: Everything pip-installable goes on PyPI proper (own platform wheels vendoring the llama.cpp DLLs and ONNX models). For weights, support three paths: huggingface_hub download from an ungated repo pinned by sha256; a documented 'download on another machine, copy the .gguf into %LOCALAPPDATA%\eMailSuperSearch\models' sideload with sha256 check; and an optional owner-hosted internal URL. Honour pip's standard proxy/cert env vars and recommend `pip install truststore` for TLS-inspected networks. Drop the first-run llama-server zip download (also an unsigned .exe launched from the profile, which AppLocker default rules block).
  * End-to-end memory budget on 16 GB UMA is never summed. 5.1 caps only the LLM (6 GB). Resident set at steady state could be: LLM 3.7-6 GB, embedder ORT session ~0.3 GB, rapidocr models + opencv ~0.5 GB, spaCy ~0.4 GB, networkx 100k nodes ~0.4 GB, SQLite page cache, the browser tab with vis-network (~0.5-1 GB), plus Outlook, Teams and Office that are always open on an office laptop (~3-4 GB), plus the iGPU frame-buffer carve-out that Windows already removed from the '15.3 GB usable'.
    why: Running ingestion (OCR + embedding) concurrently with an 8B chat model and an open graph view will swap, and swapping on a shared-memory iGPU also throttles the GPU path; 'graceful sharing' (5.1) is asserted, not designed.
    fix: Publish a memory budget table per tier and enforce it: lazy-load the LLM on first chat and unload after N idle minutes; never run LLM enrichment while chat is active; cap embedder/OCR worker count by available RAM (psutil); default to the 3-4B model when physical RAM <= 16 GB; keep the 500-node graph cap from 5.1 and apply it server-side before JSON is sent.
  * Language coverage is implicitly English-only in every default: all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (English), spaCy en_core_web_sm, FTS5 unicode61 stemming, and Windows.Media.Ocr using the user's installed profile languages. Only the OCR area mentions other languages. The PRD never states the corpus language.
    why: European corporate mailboxes are routinely bilingual; semantic recall on German or French mail with an English embedder degrades silently and the product looks 'broken' with no error.
    fix: Owner states target languages. If anything beyond English: default embedder = int8 ONNX paraphrase-multilingual-MiniLM-L12-v2 (~120 MB, still under PyPI's 100 MiB? No, split or request a limit increase) or multilingual-e5-small int8; spaCy xx_ent_wiki_sm or per-language sm models as extras; rapidocr as default OCR; project-level language setting stored with the embedder id so a re-index is triggered on change.
  * Email-specific content hygiene is absent from all areas: inline signature/logo images (cid: parts) are 'image attachments' that would be OCR'd on every single email; quoted reply chains duplicate the same text across every message in a thread (inflating the index and biasing RAG toward long threads); legal disclaimers and signatures pollute embeddings and the entity graph (every email 'mentions' the company address and phone number).
    why: Directly determines ingest time (OCR dominates per the vector-store area), index size, RAG answer quality and the usefulness of the knowledge graph.
    fix: Add a normalization stage: skip inline images referenced from HTML cid: unless above a size threshold or not referenced; dedupe quoted blocks against the parent message found via In-Reply-To/ConversationIndex (store only the new text per message, keep the full text for display); strip signatures/disclaimers with a mail-signature heuristic (e.g. talon-style rules) before embedding and NER; make all of this visible as per-file flags in the manifest.
  * Unparseable and hostile inputs have no policy: S/MIME encrypted or signed messages (smime.p7m), IRM/Azure RMS-protected mail, password-protected PDFs/ZIPs, ZIP/7z attachments containing further documents, macro-bearing Office files, legacy non-Unicode .msg, 100 MB+ attachments, PDF bombs. extract-msg will raise on some and silently return empty bodies on others.
    why: The 1:1 mirror promise means every file must end in a known state; an unhandled exception in a worker stalls the queue, and decompressing untrusted archives in a privileged user profile is a security hazard.
    fix: Manifest status enum per file and per attachment (indexed, partial, unsupported-encrypted, too-large, failed-with-reason), hard caps (attachment bytes, OCR pages, nested depth, per-file wall-clock), no archive extraction by default (opt-in with limits), and a UI badge showing how many messages are only metadata-indexed.
  * Index lifecycle is unspecified: embedder/model versioning (switching from MiniLM to a multilingual model invalidates every vector), DB schema migrations across pip upgrades, project export/import/backup, and behavior when a watched folder lives on a UNC share that is temporarily unreachable (the two-phase delete must not treat 'share offline' as 'all files deleted').
    why: The first `pip install --upgrade` or VPN drop will otherwise wipe or corrupt a user's multi-hour index.
    fix: Store embedder id + dimension + chunker version in the project header and refuse to mix; use a numbered migration table; implement 'folder root missing or stat error' as a scan abort rather than a delete pass; add project export (zip of the project directory) and import.
  * The RAG contract in 4.5 ('top 5 emails injected into the system context', cite sender and date, refuse otherwise) is not reconciled with the chunk-level retrieval all areas recommend or with the 4k-context default proposed for the 8B model. No area covers prompt design, token budgeting, chat-history handling, citation rendering, or how 'strict grounding' is tested.
    why: Five full emails with attachments can be 20k+ tokens; with a 4k context the model sees truncated garbage, and a 3-4B model is noticeably weaker at refusing than 8B. Grounding quality is the headline feature and has no acceptance test.
    fix: Specify: retrieve top-K chunks (hybrid RRF), group by email, pack by token budget (ctx minus prompt minus answer reserve) with per-email header (sender, date, subject, path) and numbered citation tags; render citations as clickable 'open in Outlook' links; keep chat history out of the model prompt beyond the last turn by default; build a 30-question golden set from a synthetic .msg corpus and measure citation accuracy and refusal rate per model tier before choosing the default model.
  * Unsigned native DLLs vs Smart App Control / WDAC was raised only for tesserocr, but the recommended stack contains more unsigned binaries: abetlen's ggml-*.dll / llama.dll (community builds), sqlite-vec's vec0.dll, vectorlite, usearch, and any vendored copies the library republishes. onnxruntime (Microsoft-signed) and msvc-runtime (Microsoft-signed) are the exceptions.
    why: On an endpoint with Smart App Control or WDAC in enforce mode the LLM engine and the vector extension both fail to load with cryptic errors, while the pure-Python parts still work; the install looks successful but core features are dead.
    fix: Doctor imports every native module in a subprocess and reports signer status (via wintrust or PowerShell Get-AuthenticodeSignature); design a 'pure-Python degraded mode' (numpy memmap brute-force vectors, no LLM, Windows OCR) so the product still searches; consider Authenticode-signing the DLLs in your own wheels if the owner has a code-signing certificate.
  * Vendoring a third-party platform wheel (llama-cpp-python) inside your own PyPI distribution is recommended but not designed: it makes the whole package win_amd64-specific (so a separate `emailsupersearch-llm-vulkan` wheel is needed to keep the core pure-Python), it collides with any user-installed `llama_cpp` package on sys.path, and each release of a 42 MB wheel per Python tag consumes PyPI's 10 GB per-project quota quickly.
    why: Packaging mistakes here break constraint 1 for every user, and PyPI quota exhaustion blocks releases.
    fix: Split distributions: `emailsupersearch` (pure Python, extras only as metadata), `emailsupersearch-engine-vulkan` and `-engine-cpu` (py3-none-win_amd64, one wheel for all Python versions as abetlen does), `emailsupersearch-models-embed` (ONNX + tokenizer), `emailsupersearch-models-ocr`. Import under your own namespace (e.g. `emailsupersearch_engine.llama_cpp`) to avoid collisions; request a PyPI project size increase early.
  * Every performance number in the digest is from Linux (RADV) or desktop CPUs; several verifiers explicitly say 'no Windows 760M figure exists' and 'must be benchmarked on the 8640HS'. No area proposes the benchmark harness, and antivirus real-time scanning of 4-5 GB GGUF reads and thousands of small .msg opens (Defender exclusions need admin) is not factored in.
    why: Default model choice, chunk size, HNSW threshold, worker counts and the 400-emails/hour enrichment claim all rest on unmeasured assumptions.
    fix: First engineering deliverable: a `emailsupersearch bench` command producing ingest files/min (parse, OCR, embed separately), query p50/p95, LLM pp/tg t/s per engine mode, peak RSS, run on the actual corporate laptop with Defender active; gate defaults on these numbers.
  * Upgrade, uninstall and multi-environment behavior is undefined: how `pip install --upgrade` interacts with the running host process holding DLLs open (Windows file locks), how a user removes all data (constraint 4 implies IT may audit the profile), and what happens when two Python installs (Store + python.org) both have the library.
    why: Locked DLLs make the upgrade fail halfway, leaving a broken venv the user cannot repair without help.
    fix: `python -m emailsupersearch stop` before upgrade (documented and attempted automatically by a tiny pure-Python updater), a `--purge` command that removes %LOCALAPPDATA%\eMailSuperSearch and the autostart entry, and a single-instance lock file so two installs never run two host processes against one project DB.


### Cross-Area Conflicts

  - chromadb: vector-store and windows-no-admin say drop it from the core and that product telemetry is a no-op since 1.5.4; msg-parsing-sync-export still recommends 'index chunks in Chroma' and says telemetry defaults to True and must be disabled with anonymized_telemetry=False; the PRD (4.3) expresses the filter DSL in Chroma syntax. One storage decision is needed and the telemetry statement must be reconciled against chromadb 1.5.9's actual code.
  - Vector/graph storage: vector-store wants SQLite + FTS5 + sqlite-vec (or numpy memmap); msg-parsing wants Chroma + FTS5 in SQLite; knowledge-graph wants graph tables in SQLite but floats Ladybug (real-ladybug) as a single store for graph + vectors + FTS. Three different persistence plans for one project directory.
  - torch: vector-store, ocr-attachments and windows-no-admin all reject torch (124 MB, MAX_PATH hazard under Store Python, no AMD GPU path); knowledge-graph's verifier recommends GLiNER2.5-small via gliner2[local], which hard-requires torch, as the preferred zero-shot extra. Either accept torch in exactly one optional extra with a MAX_PATH guard, or stick to the ONNX GLiNER build.
  - MSVC runtime remediation: llm-engine says ship msvcp140/vcruntime140/vcruntime140_1 app-locally inside the wheel (Microsoft redist terms; needs a licensed VS user to redistribute) or build a static-CRT wheel; windows-no-admin says `pip install msvc-runtime` into the BASE interpreter (not the venv) and that the doctor should only suggest it; ocr-attachments relies on onnxruntime, which also needs the CRT, without mentioning it. msvc-runtime has no cp311 wheel, so the two plans also disagree implicitly on the Python floor.
  - Python version floor: knowledge-graph pins networkx<3.7 to keep 3.11; ocr-attachments declares 3.11-3.14; web-ui's Starlette 1.9 needs >=3.11; windows-no-admin's msvc-runtime needs >=3.12; pywin32 and real-ladybug stop at 3.13; winrt stops at 3.14. No area owns the final range.
  - Background sync mechanism: the PRD says Task Scheduler + pythonw; web-ui says a user-level `schtasks /rl LIMITED` task works with a Startup-folder shortcut as fallback; windows-no-admin says `schtasks /sc ONLOGON` is reported to fail non-elevated and S4U is unavailable to Users, so use an in-process watcher + Startup shortcut; both areas float HKCU\...\Run as 'unverified'. Pick one autostart mechanism and verify it on the target.
  - Process topology: windows-no-admin runs the file watcher inside the web-server process; ocr-attachments requires OCR backends to be isolated in a separate worker process after reproducible segfaults when winrt OCR and rapidocr/onnxruntime share a process; knowledge-graph's LLM enrichment and web-ui's LLM chat stream share one GPU and one model object. Nobody defines the supervisor/queue that makes these coexist.
  - onnxruntime vs onnxruntime-directml: the core embedder (vector-store), rapidocr (ocr-attachments) and the doctor all assume the CPU `onnxruntime` package, while vector-store [gpu], ocr-attachments [ocr-gpu] and windows-no-admin's GenAI-DirectML alternative each propose installing `onnxruntime-directml`. Both distributions install the same top-level `onnxruntime` import package into site-packages (pip overwrites whichever came first) and the ORT docs do not warn about it (checked https://onnxruntime.ai/docs/install/ and the DirectML EP page). The extras as proposed cannot be combined; a DirectML tier must replace, not add to, onnxruntime, or live in its own subprocess/venv.
  - Default LLM: the PRD mandates Llama 3 8B Instruct Q4_0; llm-engine defaults to a 3-4B Apache-licensed model (Qwen3-4B) with 8B optional at 4k context; knowledge-graph's throughput and GraphRAG numbers assume 8B; PRD 5.1's 6 GB cap is violated by 8B at 8k. The model tier must be fixed before any RAG/enrichment design.
  - LLM engine fallback: llm-engine's verifier suggests downloading the official ggml-org Windows Vulkan zip at first run and running llama-server as a subprocess; windows-no-admin says AppLocker default rules block any .exe launched from the user profile and that nothing beyond pip may be downloaded; constraint 1 forbids extra downloads. The zip fallback should be dropped or made an IT-deployed option.
  - Excel export: PRD 4.4 says pandas + openpyxl; msg-parsing recommends openpyxl directly with no pandas; its verifier suggests XlsxWriter; vector-store's core excludes pandas entirely. One export library should be chosen (and pandas removed from the core if it is not needed elsewhere).
  - Knowledge-graph output: PRD 4.6 wants a standalone pyvis knowledge_map.html; knowledge-graph says pyvis is abandoned and loads CDNs in every mode, so the graph must be a vis-network view inside the web UI fed by JSON; web-ui's static-asset budget (2-5 MB) does not mention the graph bundle or the 500-node cap from 5.1. Both areas agree on the fix but neither owns the UI integration.
  - .msg parser licence: msg-parsing keeps extract-msg (GPL-3.0) behind an adapter and only lists python-oxmsg (MIT, 0.0.2, no RTF de-encapsulation) as an alternative; ocr-attachments' verifier says the product owner must consciously accept GPL-3 or switch to python-oxmsg; no area addresses what a GPL dependency means for the licence of a PyPI package the owner may want to keep proprietary or permissive.
  - Chunking vs the PRD: the PRD (4.2) appends attachment text to the body and embeds one vector per email; msg-parsing and vector-store both say this is broken by MiniLM's 256-token truncation and require chunk-level indexing with parent grouping; the PRD's 'top 5 emails' RAG contract (4.5) and Chroma-style metadata filters (4.3) were written for the one-vector-per-email model and are not re-specified for chunks anywhere.
  - OCR default: ocr-attachments makes Windows.Media.Ocr the default even though Microsoft documents it as supported only for MSIX-packaged apps; windows-no-admin's doctor and wheel audits never list the six winrt-* wheels (compiled C++/WinRT .pyd files that also need the MSVC CRT and may be affected by WDAC); the two areas need a shared native-module inventory.
  - Telemetry statements: llm-engine says onnxruntime and huggingface_hub phone home by default and must be disabled by env var; vector-store and ocr-attachments adopt onnxruntime for embeddings and OCR without mentioning ORT's Windows telemetry (disable_telemetry_events) or HF_HUB_DISABLE_TELEMETRY; constraint 4 requires a single, complete zero-network checklist.


### Top Design Decisions For Owner

  * What a 'project' is and how it maps to storage and sync
    options: Project = saved search/filters over one shared global index of all watched folders | Project = its own watched folder set + its own index, graph, chats and exports (isolated SQLite file per project) | Hybrid: shared parsed-message cache keyed by file hash, per-project index/graph/chat built from a folder selection
    default: Hybrid: a global parse/OCR cache (so one .msg is parsed and OCR'd once) with one SQLite file per project holding manifest, FTS5, vectors, graph tables and chat history; projects can share folders; this keeps the 1:1 mirror cheap and makes project export/delete trivial.
  * Supported Python versions for v1
    options: 3.11-3.14 (widest, but msvc-runtime remediation and networkx 3.7 unavailable on 3.11; pywin32/real-ladybug missing on 3.14) | 3.12-3.13 only (every proposed wheel exists) | 3.13 only
    default: Requires-Python >=3.12,<3.14 with a CI job that resolves every extra as wheels-only on win_amd64; widen later when msvc-runtime and all native deps cover more versions.
  * Default LLM model and its licence
    options: Llama 3.1 8B Instruct Q4_K_M at 4k context (PRD intent; Llama 3.1 community licence with 'Built with Llama' attribution; official repo gated, third-party GGUF repos ungated) | Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 Q4_K_M (Apache-2.0, ~3.7 GB at 8k, faster, fits the 6 GB cap with margin) | Llama-3.2-3B-Instruct Q4_K_M (~3.0 GB, Llama licence)
    default: Qwen3-4B-Instruct-2507 Q4_K_M as the shipped default (Apache-2.0 removes the Meta licence and gating questions, fits 16 GB alongside Outlook/Teams); Llama 3.1 8B as an optional download with 4k context or q8 KV cache, selectable per project.
  * LLM engine packaging and GPU policy
    options: Vendor abetlen's llama-cpp-python Vulkan + CPU builds into own PyPI platform wheels, choose GPU/CPU at runtime | Depend on llama-cpp-python via the GitHub Pages extra index (breaks pip-mirror-only networks) | CPU-only engine in v1 (ik-llama-cpp-python or vendored CPU wheel), Vulkan as a later opt-in
    default: Own PyPI wheels `emailsupersearch-engine-vulkan` and `-engine-cpu` (py3-none-win_amd64, pinned llama.cpp commit, app-local Microsoft CRT DLLs), runtime probe selects Vulkan then host-memory Vulkan then CPU; the user never chooses a GPU extra.
  * Licence of the library and acceptance of GPL/AGPL dependencies
    options: Accept GPL-3 (extract-msg) and release the library under a GPL-compatible licence | Use MIT python-oxmsg as default parser and make extract-msg an opt-in extra (RTF-only bodies, legacy encodings), keeping the library permissive | Write/own an MIT .msg parser (CFB + MAPI properties + RTF de-encapsulation) to remove the dependency
    default: Permissive library licence; python-oxmsg as the default parser with a parity test suite on real corpora, extract-msg behind an explicit `[gpl-parser]` extra that the installer warns about; PyMuPDF (AGPL) and leidenalg (GPL) excluded outright.
  * Vector and metadata storage engine
    options: SQLite only: stdlib FTS5 + sqlite-vec brute force (or fp16 numpy memmap), usearch/vectorlite tier above ~500k chunks | chromadb PersistentClient with own embedder (85 packages, Rust binding needs VC++ runtime, no local hybrid search) | LanceDB (real SQL WHERE + FTS, 129 MB) or Ladybug (graph + vectors + FTS in one embedded DB)
    default: SQLite-only core (FTS5 + sqlite-vec, metadata filters in SQL passed as rowid IN), behind a small VectorStore interface so a [chroma] or [lancedb] backend can be added; graph nodes/edges in the same SQLite file.
  * Target corpus languages
    options: English only (MiniLM-L6 int8, spaCy en_core_web_sm, Windows OCR en) | English + one or more European languages (multilingual MiniLM-L12/e5-small int8, per-language spaCy, rapidocr default) | Any language incl. CJK
    default: Ask the owner; if unspecified, ship English-only models in the base wheel and a `[multilingual]` models wheel, with language stored per project and a forced re-index on change.
  * Knowledge-graph construction method
    options: Deterministic metadata graph (sender/recipients/threads/attachments/domains) + spaCy NER + co-mention edges; LLM triple extraction opt-in, resumable, per project | PRD as written: LLM triple extraction for every email during ingestion (~42-67 h per 20k emails on the 760M) | Deterministic graph + GLiNER2 zero-shot NER/relations (requires torch in an extra)
    default: Deterministic + spaCy en_core_web_sm by default with EntityRuler patterns for office identifiers; LLM enrichment as an opt-in background job that runs only when chat is idle and shows progress; GLiNER only if the owner accepts torch in one extra.
  * Background sync and autostart mechanism
    options: Always-on host process (web server + job queue + watcher) auto-started via per-user Startup-folder shortcut or HKCU Run, workers as subprocesses | PRD approach: Task Scheduler user task launching pythonw.exe on logon | Sync only while the UI is open (no autostart)
    default: Always-on host process with watchfiles + periodic reconcile, autostart via Startup folder (HKCU Run as alternative), both toggleable in Settings; Task Scheduler offered only as an IT-deployable option after on-target verification.
  * OCR default and inline-image policy
    options: Windows.Media.Ocr (0.9 MB, ~0.1 s/page, formally MSIX-only) with rapidocr fallback in a separate worker | rapidocr always (110 MB extra, ~3 s/page, fully supported, multilingual) | No OCR in the base install; `[ocr]` extra only
    default: Windows.Media.Ocr best-effort default with automatic rapidocr fallback, both run in an isolated OCR worker process; skip cid:-referenced inline images below a size threshold; OCR page and time caps per attachment.
  * Model-weight distribution
    options: huggingface_hub download from pinned ungated repos with sha256, HF_HUB_OFFLINE afterwards | Sideload only (user copies .gguf/.onnx into the models folder; no network code in the library) | Owner-hosted internal URL or file share as the primary source
    default: All three supported, with the UI offering download first, then a 'copy file here' sideload with sha256 verification; embedding and OCR models ship inside PyPI wheels so only LLM weights ever need a download.
  * Scale target for v1
    options: Up to 100k emails / ~300k chunks (brute-force vectors, networkx in memory) | Up to 1M emails (needs HNSW tier, graph in SQLite only, sharded FTS) | Unbounded
    default: Design for 100k emails per project, verify by benchmark on the 8640HS, and put usearch/vectorlite behind a `[bigindex]` extra that activates automatically past a measured threshold.
  * Excel export implementation and the file:// hyperlink promise
    options: openpyxl only (read/write, needed if .xlsx attachments are read anyway) | XlsxWriter (default hyperlink style, autofit, URL-length guard; write-only) | pandas + openpyxl as the PRD states (adds pandas 10+ MB and numpy coupling)
    default: openpyxl (already required for .xlsx attachment parsing) with Path.as_uri() hyperlinks plus a plain path column; the in-app 'Open in Outlook' button is the primary action and the docs state that Office may show a hyperlink warning that only the user (HKCU DisableHyperlinkWarning) or IT can suppress.
  * Memory and GPU governance between ingestion and chat
    options: LLM resident while the app runs; ingestion throttled | LLM loaded on first chat, unloaded after idle timeout; enrichment paused on chat | Separate CPU LLM for enrichment, GPU LLM for chat
    default: Lazy load + idle unload (configurable), one LLM worker that serves chat or enrichment but never both, OCR/embedding worker count derived from free RAM; publish the budget table in the docs.
  * How the MSVC C++ runtime dependency is satisfied for a standard user
    options: Ship msvcp140/vcruntime140/vcruntime140_1 app-locally inside the engine wheel (Microsoft redistribution terms) | Instruct the doctor to run `pip install msvc-runtime` into the base interpreter (cp312+ only) | Build static-CRT wheels for all vendored native code
    default: Detect first (most Office/Teams images already have VC++ 2015-2022 x64); if missing, ship app-local CRT DLLs next to the engine DLLs loaded via os.add_dll_directory, and have the doctor offer msvc-runtime as a second remedy; static-CRT only for binaries the project builds itself.


### Prd Items At Risk

  - Section 3 / 4.5 / 5.1 'gpt4all' with device="amd": the PyPI package is frozen at 2.8.2 (2024-08-14), the project is unmaintained since the 2025-02 app release, its Kompute/Vulkan path accelerates only Q4_0/Q4_1 and is unbenchmarked on the 760M, and it needs msvcp140/vcruntime140 that a standard user cannot install. Cannot be met as written; replace with llama-cpp-python (Vulkan/CPU) and drop the device="amd" wording for a runtime-probed backend.
  - 5.1 'LLM must not exceed 6 GB' together with 'Llama 3 8B Instruct Q4_0': 8B Q4 weights (4.66-4.92 GB) plus an f16 KV cache at 8k context is ~5.7-6.0 GB before compute buffers, i.e. at or over the cap on a 16 GB UMA machine that also runs Outlook and a browser. Meetable only with 4k context / q8 KV or a 3-4B model.
  - 4.6 'Use the local LLM during the ingestion pipeline to parse email bodies and extract RDF-style relationships': measured-proxy throughput is ~300-480 emails/hour on the iGPU (42-67 h per 20k emails) and ~160-210/h on CPU; as the default ingestion step it is not viable and collides with 5.1's 'prevent locking up the local machine' because the iGPU also drives the display. Must become opt-in/resumable enrichment.
  - 4.6 'pyvis ... saved locally as knowledge_map.html for offline browser exploration': pyvis 0.3.2 is unmaintained (Feb 2023) and loads vis-network/Bootstrap from CDNs in every mode except in_line, so the file is blank offline. Replace with vis-network bundled in the library's own web UI.
  - 4.1 'filename-based Python set mathematics': Outlook names dropped files by subject, stamps mtime with the drop time, prompts to overwrite same-subject files, and re-exports are byte-different; identity must come from Message-ID or a metadata hash in a manifest with periodic full reconciliation. Also, new Outlook drag-drop produces .eml, not .msg, so a .msg-only mirror silently misses mail.
  - 4.2 'easyocr user-cached model' and 'PyMuPDF': easyocr pulls ~250 MB of wheels including torch, downloads 91-106 MB of weights from GitHub on first use (breaks 'nothing but pip' and offline), has no AMD GPU path and its last release is Sep 2024; PyMuPDF 2.0 is AGPL-3/commercial and 63 MB. Replace with Windows.Media.Ocr / rapidocr and pypdfium2.
  - 4.2 'Append all extracted attachment text directly to the parent email's body text prior to embedding': the default all-MiniLM-L6-v2 ONNX embedder truncates at 256 word pieces, so appended attachment text is never embedded and the requirement defeats itself. Must be chunk-level indexing with parent grouping.
  - 4.3 'ChromaDB's MongoDB-style syntax ($gte, $lte, $and, $or)': if chromadb leaves the core (recommended by two areas: 85 packages, S3 model download, VC++ runtime import failure on a fresh Windows 11 25H2) the DSL must be re-implemented over SQL; and Chroma's own hybrid Search/Rrf API is Cloud-only, so Chroma could not deliver fused keyword+vector search offline anyway.
  - 4.4 'pandas and openpyxl ... file:/// hyperlinks allowing users to launch the original .msg file in Outlook directly from the spreadsheet': Office shows a 'Hyperlinks can be harmful' prompt for local file links unless HKCU DisableHyperlinkWarning is set (and policy can override it), and whether classic or new Outlook opens the .msg depends on the file association; pandas adds a large dependency for formatting that openpyxl/XlsxWriter do directly. Deliverable as 'links that work with a warning' plus an in-app Open-in-Outlook action.
  - 4.5 'Retrieve the top 5 most semantically relevant emails ... inject them into the LLM's system context window': five whole emails with attachment text exceed a 4k (and often an 8k) context; the requirement must be restated as chunk retrieval packed to a token budget.
  - 4.7 'pythonw.exe orchestrated by Windows Task Scheduler (triggering only when the user is logged on)': creating ONLOGON tasks non-elevated is reported to fail, S4U/password tasks need the 'Log on as a batch job' right Users lack, Task Scheduler GPOs may block user tasks, and pythonw.exe has no stdout/stderr so native-library logging crashes the process. Use a per-user Startup entry or HKCU Run plus explicit fd redirection, with Task Scheduler as an unverified optional path.
  - 5.2 'ML model weights (~/.cache/gpt4all)' and 'databases (./email_vector_db)': a relative path depends on the CWD of whatever launched the process and ~/.cache is gpt4all-specific; both must become %LOCALAPPDATA%\eMailSuperSearch\... (non-roaming, not Controlled-Folder-Access protected).
  - 5.2 'Zero Telemetry ... ChromaDB, EasyOCR, and GPT4All must operate strictly within the offline localhost environment': as shipped, chromadb's default embedding function downloads an 83 MB archive from S3 on first use, EasyOCR downloads weights from GitHub, gpt4all downloads models and model lists, huggingface_hub and onnxruntime (Windows builds) emit telemetry by default, and pyvis loads CDNs. The requirement is meetable only by replacing/configuring every one of these; it needs a single enforced zero-network checklist and an automated test that runs the full stack with networking blocked.
  - Constraint 1 'The ONLY thing the end user installs is this library via pip': llama-cpp-python's Windows wheels are not on PyPI (GitHub Pages extra index), LLM weights cannot be on PyPI (100 MiB default / 1 GiB hard per-file limit), Windows OCR language packs are Windows Features, and the MSVC C++ runtime is not pip-installable for Python 3.11. Meetable only via own PyPI platform wheels with vendored DLLs/CRT, a separate weights download/sideload step the user must accept, and a Python 3.12+ floor.
  - Constraint 4 'no admin rights' interacting with the stack: a missing VC++ runtime is a hard ImportError for torch/onnxruntime/chromadb/llama.cpp and vc_redist needs UAC; AppLocker default rules block a per-user python.exe entirely (Store Python excepted); WDAC/Smart App Control can block unsigned ggml/sqlite-vec DLLs; GPU driver updates are impossible. The library can only detect and degrade, not guarantee, so the PRD's 'zero-admin' promise needs an explicit degraded-mode definition.
  - Section 2 'AMD Radeon 760M' + 5.1 'Vulkan GPU Offloading': the BIOS UMA frame buffer on corporate laptops is typically 512 MB-2 GB and locked; llama.cpp's Vulkan allocation behavior on UMA devices changed in June 2026 (PR #22930, later reverted by some forks) and has no Windows/AMD-APU documentation, so whether a 3.7-4.9 GB model loads on the iGPU, and at what speed under the Windows AMD driver, is unverified. Treat GPU acceleration as best-effort with CPU fallback until measured on the target.
  - Constraint 5 '1:1 mirror' plus 4.1 'Automated Pruning': with watchdog/watchfiles losing events on buffer overflow and UNC shares going offline on VPN drops, an eager delete-on-missing implementation will purge valid records; mirroring is meetable only with two-phase deletes, a root-reachability check, and periodic full reconciliation.
